AWS SAA-C03 Architecture & Services Handbook

IAM

AWS Global Infrastructure fundamentals

Purpose: Understand where AWS places resources to choose the right deployment location, reduce latency, and avoid service outages when problems occur.

1. Region and Availability Zone

Region: a geographical area, for example Singapore.

Each Region has many Availability Zones (AZ); Each AZ includes one or more data centers with independent power sources and connections.

AZs in the same Region are connected by a low latency network.
EC2, EBS and subnet are in one AZ; Other services may have a Region scope
or global.

2. Multi-AZ and Multi-Region

Multi-AZ: run the application in multiple AZs in the same Region. If one AZ fails, the other AZ will continue to serve, if the application still has enough capacity and the data is protected.

Multi-Region: Deploy in many Regions to serve global users or restore when the whole Region has problems.

Must consider additional costs and ways to synchronize data.

User → Load Balancer
                ├─ EC2 is in AZ A
                └─ EC2 in AZ B

3. Select Region

Consider the user's location, what country the data request must be in, whether the service needed is available and the cost.

Edge location is used to bring content/services closer to users; not an AZ to place EC2 yourself.

AZ names like ap-southeast-1a may map differently between accounts. When you need to compare the same physical location between accounts, use AZ ID.

4. Use cases

Use cases: The website needs to tolerate one data center: ALB and EC2/ASG across at least two AZs, the database has Multi-AZ. Global application needs to recover when Region fails: add DR architecture in another Region.

Quick review · SAA-C03

Region is a geographical area; AZ is a separate error domain in Region. Multi-AZ handles AZ errors, while Multi-Region extends recovery capabilities when the entire Region fails.

IAM identities: Root, Users & Groups

Purpose: Determine who is using AWS and manage the rights of each person in the account.

1. AWS account and root user

ConceptNature & Scope
AWS AccountThe isolation unit contains resources and billing on AWS
Root UserThe account's special identity has very broad permissions, only used for tasks that require root privileges

Turn on MFA for root, protect recovery information, do not create root access keys and do not use root for daily work.

Do not treat the root user as a way to bypass all protection mechanisms: SCP and some mechanisms such as S3 Object Lock Compliance can still limit operations.

2. IAM Users and Groups

IAM IdentityFeatures & Rules
IAM UserLong-term identity; can have password (to Console) and/or access key (API/CLI call — two separate credential types)
IAM GroupGroup IAM Users to grant common permissions; A user can belong to many groups, but the group cannot nest another group and does not contain an IAM Role
Group Developers → policy allows viewing EC2
                 ├─ User An
                 └─ User Binh

3. Login for employees

Prioritize IAM Identity Center or federation: employees log in and receive access to roles with temporary credentials.

No need to create and share long-term access keys for each person.

If using IAM User, log in with account ID/alias, user name and password. Each session only has permissions for the identity/role in use.

4. IAM is global in scope

No need to recreate IAM User in each Region. However, the accessed resource may be located in a specific Region and the policy may limit that scope.

5. Use cases

Use cases: The audit team uses read-only roles; The development team has limited permissions according to the environment. Use groups to manage common rights for existing IAM Users; Use Identity Center to manage employee access across multiple accounts.

Quick review · SAA-C03

IAM User is a long-term identity; Group collects Users; IAM Role grants permissions through a temporary session. Employees with multiple accounts should prioritize Identity Center.

IAM policies, Roles & permission model

Purpose: Grant the right permissions, on the right resources, without having to share passwords or access keys.

1. Read a policy

Policy: JSON rule set.

Each Statement answers: allow or prohibit, what to do, on what resources and under what conditions.

FieldMeaning
EffectAllow or Deny
ActionAPI/action, e.g. s3:GetObject
ResourceResource ARN; object ARN is different from bucket ARN
ConditionConditions like MFA, IP or tag
PrincipalWho is authorized; appears in resource/trust policy, not in identity policy

The commonly used version is 2012-10-17; This is the policy language version. For example, the identity policy only allows reading objects in a prefix:

{"Version":"2012-10-17","Statement":[{"Effect":"Allow","Action":"s3:GetObject","Resource":"arn:aws:s3:::example-bucket/reports/*"}]}

2. Identity policy and resource policy

Identity policy attached to user/group/role: what does this identity do? A resource policy is attached to a bucket, queue or key and specifies who may access that resource.

With direct cross-account access, Allow is often needed on both the identity and
resource side.
KMS also has its own key policy; Having S3 permissions may not be able to decrypt the object
SSE-KMS.

3. How to consider rights

If there is no appropriate Allow, it will be denied by default.

Explicit Deny wins over Allow.

Rights are also limited by SCP, permissions boundary or session policy where those mechanisms apply; they are not self-authorizing.

Least privilege means only granting actions and resources that are truly needed, instead of using * for everything.

4. Role and STS

Role is an identity that is assumed when needed.
Trust policy determines who gets assumed; Permission policy determines what role to do
what.
STS issues temporary access keys, secret keys and session tokens.
EC2/Lambda/user → assume role → temporary credential → call AWS API

IAM Roles Anywhere

IAM Roles Anywhere for the server, container or application to run outside of AWS Use IAM roles to receive temporary credentials, instead of keeping long-term access keys.

Other on-premises / cloud applications + X.509 certificate
↓ authenticate with the registered CA as trust anchor
IAM Roles Anywhere + IAM roles / profiles
↓
Temporary credentials
↓
AWS API according to role permissions

Requires a valid certificate from a trusted CA and an appropriate role trust policy. For example, on-premises server uploads backup to S3.

5. IAM Roles for AWS services

IAM RoleWhat to use?
EC2 Instance RoleApplications on EC2 call S3, DynamoDB, SQS...; attach the role to EC2 via instance profile
ECS Task RoleApplications in ECS containers call AWS APIs
ECS Task Execution RoleECS agent/Fargate pulls image from ECR, sends container logs to CloudWatch Logs
Lambda Execution RoleLambda function calls AWS APIs and records logs according to the policy granted
EKS Pod Identity RoleGrant AWS permissions to Pods via association with the Kubernetes Service Account and Pod Identity Agent
IRSA Role (EKS)Pods assume roles via Kubernetes Service Account, IAM OIDC provider and STS
Step Functions Execution RoleWorkflow calls Lambda, ECS, DynamoDB... according to granted permissions
CloudFormation Service RoleCloudFormation creates, edits, and deletes stack AWS resources
Cross-Account IAM RolePrincipal in another account assumes role to access resources according to trust/permission policies
Service-Linked RoleAn AWS service manages the necessary resources for you
EC2 Application
└── EC2 Instance Role → S3

ECS Containers
├── Task Role → App calls S3
└── Task Execution Role → ECS pull ECR / send logs

Lambda Function
└── Lambda Execution Role → DynamoDB

EKS Pod
└── Pod Identity / IRSA Role → S3

Step Functions
└── Execution Role → Lambda

CloudFormation
└── Service Role → Create EC2 / RDS

ECS Task Role grants permissions to applications; Task Execution Role grants permissions to ECS agent/Fargate. Pod Identity and IRSA are two ways to grant AWS permissions to Pods; Details and conditions are in the EKS section. ·

6. Service-Linked Role

Service-Linked Role (SLR) is a type of service role that is specifically associated with an AWS service. That service assumes the role to operate on AWS resources for you; AWS defines the necessary trust and permissions.

For example, Auto Scaling Group needs permission to launch, terminate EC2, and register/unregister instances with the load balancer. SLR provides these rights; The EC2 Instance Role remains application-specific within EC2.

AWS serviceSLR is used for
EC2 Auto ScalingManage EC2 lifecycle and register instances with load balancer
Elastic Load BalancingManage AWS resources needed by the load balancer, such as network interfaces
Application Auto ScalingScale the resources of supported services, for example ECS service or DynamoDB

Many services automatically create SLRs when needed if the configurator has permission iam:CreateServiceLinkedRole. Do not manually edit permissions managed by the service. · ·

7. Use cases

Use cases: EC2 reads an S3 prefix using the instance role; Lambda logs using execution role. Deploy to another account using AssumeRole, with appropriate trust policy and assume rights on both sides.

8. Inline Policy vs Managed Policy & Identity Aggregation

CriteriaInline PolicyManaged Policy
Nature & ClassificationPolicy is tied directly to a single identityPolicy exists independently: including AWS Managed (eg AdministratorAccess Omnipotence is different from SystemAdministrator limited administrative rights) and Customer Managed (you create and manage versions yourself)
Mounting rangeA single User / Group / RoleReusable for many Users / Groups / Roles
Lifecycle when deleting an identityDelete by ownership identityMaintain independence within IAM
When to use?1–1 rights are uniquely tied to an identityRight to standardize, reuse, and version control
ComponentNatureCredential used
IAM UserLong-term identity in accountPassword (Console) or Access Key ID + long-term Secret Access Key
IAM RoleThe temporary identity is assumedSTS Temporary Credentials (Access Key + Secret Key + Session Token)
IAM PolicyJSON document specifies Allow / DenyTrust Policy (who can assume) · Permission Policy (what can be done)
Quick review · SAA-C03

If there is no Allow, it will be Deny; Explicit Deny always wins. Roles need both a trust policy to be assumed and a permission policy to perform actions.

IAM credentials, authentication & security tools

Purpose: Log in and call AWS securely, and find credentials or permissions that are no longer needed.

Two main ways of access:

Console → password + MFA
CLI / SDK → credentials → AWS API

1. Console, CLI and SDK

Console
= browser interface; CLI runs command; SDK calls AWS from code.

CLI/SDK uses temporary credential from SSO/role or long-term access key, IAM User access key is not required.

Temporary credentials include access key ID, secret access key and session token. Do not hard-code credentials into the application; Do not commit the credentials file.

2. MFA and password policy

MFA requires additional authentication factors in addition to passwords.

Prioritize MFA for root and high privileges.

IAM's password policy controls IAM User password length, complexity, expiration, and reuse; Does not replace MFA and does not apply to all federation systems.

3. Profile and CloudShell

CLI profile separates configuration between accounts/roles. aws configure sso set up SSO; aws configure can store access keys in ~/.aws/credentials and Region configuration in ~/.aws/config.

CloudShell: terminal in Console, has CLI available and uses current login session permissions.

It does not grant itself administrative rights.

4. Permission checking tool

ToolsUsed to
Credentials ReportView IAM User password, access key and MFA status for the entire account
Access AdvisorView service usage information to consider narrowing permissions
IAM Access AnalyzerIAM Access Analyzer is a service that helps analyze AWS access permissions, detecting permissions that are too broad, permissions that are not in use, or resources that may be accessed unintentionally.

Usage data is the basis for review, not the reason for immediate removal: some tasks only run monthly or when DR.

5. Use cases

Use cases: Employees use SSO profiles for CLI; workload uses role. Check access keys that have not been used for a long time, delete excess keys and reduce permissions after checking actual needs.

Quick review · SAA-C03

Console uses interactive login; CLI/SDK can use temporary credentials. Access Analyzer checks permissions; Credentials Report checks IAM User information.

IAM - Advanced

AWS Organizations & Service Control Policies (SCP)

Purpose: AWS Organizations is used to manage multiple AWS accounts within the same organization, helping to separate workload, billing, and administration rights.

AWS Organizations = Multi-Account Management + Consolidated Billing + OU + SCP

1. Management Account & Member Account

Management Account = Organization management account

Member Account = member accounts

For example:

Management Account
├── Dev Account
├── Test Account
├── Prod Account
└── Finance Account

One member account belongs only one Organization at a time.

2. Why use multiple accounts?

Instead:

1 AWS Account
├── Dev
├── Test
├── Prod
└── Finance

split into: Dev Account · Test Account · Prod Account · Finance Account

Help: Separation of rights · Separation of billing · Separation of workload · Reduce blast radius

Dev encountered a security issue
↓
reduce the risk of direct effects Prod

3. Consolidated Billing

Organizations can pool costs across multiple accounts:

Dev
Test
Prod
Finance
↓
Consolidated Billing
↓
1 bill chung

Benefits: 1 payment method · Central cost tracking · Volume discount · Savings Plans / RI sharing

4. Organizational Unit - OU

OU = group of AWS accounts with the same policy/governance.

Organization Root
├── Development OU
│   ├── Dev Account A
│   └── Dev Account B
│
└── Production OU
    ├── HR Account
    └── Finance Account

OUs can be nested:

Root
↓
Production OU
↓
Finance OU
↓
Finance Account

OU = account group to manage policy, not network/VPC.

5. Service Control Policy - SCP

SCP = maximum permission limit that the account is allowed to use.

SCP = Maximum Permission Boundary at the Organization level

Important: SCP ≠ grant permissions

SCP = limit permissions

IAM · Allow s3:*

but SCP: Deny s3:*

S3 ❌

Explicit Deny in SCP wins over IAM Allow.

6. Where can SCP be mounted?

Root · OU · Account

Root
↓
Sandbox OU
↓
SCP: Deny s3:*
↓
Account A
Account B

→ both Account A and B are blocked from S3.

7. SCP Evaluation

When user/role calls API:

Request
↓
Does SCP have Deny?
↓
Does SCP give action?
↓
Does IAM / Resource Policy Allow?
↓
Allow / Deny

To request successfully:

SCP
→ must allow

IAM
→ must also allow

8. Blocklist Strategy

FullAWSAccess + Deny unwanted services

Allow Everything
↓
Deny DynamoDB

EC2 ✅ · S3 ✅ · Lambda ✅ · DynamoDB ❌

Blocklist = for many, block some.

9. Allowlist Strategy

Only Allow the services you need to use

Allow EC2 · Allow CloudWatch

EC2 ✅ · CloudWatch ✅ · S3 ❌ · Lambda ❌ · DynamoDB ❌

Allowlist = only for what is listed.

10. SCP Inheritance

SCP applies along the following lines:

Root
↓
OU cha
↓
OU con
↓
Account
Root
Allow EC2 + S3
↓
Production OU
Allow EC2
↓
Account A

EC2 ✅ · S3 ❌

Because the parent OU did not give S3.

Account below cannot reopen permissions that have been limited by the parent OU.

11. Tag Policy

Tag Policy is used to standardize tags in Organization.

Environment = Dev / Test / Prod

or: CostCenter = 100 / 200

Used for: Billing · Cost Allocation · Audit · Resource Classification · ABAC

12. Tag Policy vs IAM

Tag Policy = tag must be correct

IAM = user/role has permission to call the API or not

Tag Policy
→ Environment can only be Dev/Test/Prod

IAM can use tags to control:

User Project=A
↓
Manage Resource Project=A

ABAC = Attribute-Based Access Control

13. Centralized Logging

There is usually a separate account for logging/security:

Dev
Prod
Finance
↓
CloudTrail / CloudWatch Logs
↓
Central Logging Account

Security teams can:

Assume Cross-Account Role
↓
Investigate other accounts

14. Use Cases

Use cases:

Development OU
↓
policy is more open
Production OU
↓
SCP is tighter
Sandbox OU
↓
Deny service is sensitive / expensive
Multiple Accounts
↓
Consolidated Billing
↓
Central Finance
Quick review · SAA-C03

SCP sets a ceiling on permissions for accounts/OUs, not Allow itself; management accounts and member accounts have different administrative scopes.

IAM policy evaluation, boundaries & cross-account access

Purpose: Understand how AWS combines multiple policy layers to decide whether a final request is Allow or Deny.

Default = Implicit Deny

Explicit Deny = wins all Allow

For example:

Deny sqs:*
+
Allow sqs:DeleteQueue
↓
DeleteQueue ❌

Just have Explicit Deny matches → request is blocked.

1. Main types of Policy

Identity-based Policy = attached to User / Group / Role = what does principal do?

Resource-based Policy = attached to resource = who can access the resource

Resource policy example: S3 Bucket Policy · SQS Queue Policy · SNS Topic Policy · Lambda Resource Policy

2. SCP, Boundary, Session Policy

  • SCP = limit maximum permissions to Organization / OU / Account
  • Permission Boundary = limit the maximum permissions of IAM User / Role
  • Session Policy = narrow temporary session permissions

Important: SCP / Boundary / Session Policy ≠ self-authorization

They only limit rights

3. Effective Permission

Can simply remember:

Identity Allow
∩
Permission Boundary
∩
SCP
∩
Session Policy (if any)
↓
Effective Permission

and:

Explicit Deny
→ DENY now
Identity Policy:
Allow iam:CreateUser

Boundaries:
Allow s3:*
Allow ec2:*

↓
There is no delivery
↓
CreateUser ❌

Boundary Allow a service does not mean providing that service yourself.

4. IAM Conditions

Condition adds context to the decision.

Can check: MFA · IP Address · VPC Endpoint · Principal Tag · Resource Tag · Organization ID

5. ABAC

ABAC = Attribute-Based Access Control

User: · Department = Data · EC2: · Project = DataAnalytics

Policy can give:

Data Team
↓
Start/Stop only
↓
EC2 has Project=DataAnalytics

→ tag-based management instead of creating separate policies for each user.

6. MFA Condition

MFA = false
↓
Deny ec2:TerminateInstances

Even if the user has: Allow ec2:*

then: Terminate EC2 ❌

because Explicit Deny wins.

7. S3 Bucket ARN vs Object ARN

Very common mistake:

s3:ListBucket
→ arn:aws:s3:::my-bucket
s3:GetObject
s3:PutObject
s3:DeleteObject
→ arn:aws:s3:::my-bucket/*

Remember: Bucket = bucket ARN

Object = bucket ARN + /*

8. aws

Bucket Policy can only be accessed by accounts belonging to the same AWS Organization.

S3 Bucket
↓
Condition:
aws:PrincipalOrgID = o-123456
Account in Organization
→ ✅

Account outside the Organization
→ ❌

→ no need to list each AWS account in the policy.

9. Cross-Account Access

There are 2 popular ways.

9.1. AssumeRole

Principal Account A
↓
AssumeRole
↓
Role Account B
↓
Temporary Credentials

Principal operates under the authority of role session.

Suitable when needed: many resources · many AWS services · a separate set of permissions at Account B

9.2. Resource-Based Policy

Principal Account A
↓
Resource Policy in Account B
↓
Resource
User Account A
↓
PutObject
↓
S3 Bucket Account B

User still retains original identity/authority in Account A.

Suitable for just sharing: S3 Bucket · SQS Queue · SNS Topic · ...

AssumeRole = change to role session. Resource Policy = current principal accesses resources directly.

10. Permission Boundary Use Case

Use cases:

Boundary is very useful when allowing developers to create their own IAM roles/users.

Developer
↓
Create Role
↓
Permission Boundary is required

The boundary example shows: S3 · EC2 · CloudWatch

Developer even attach: AdministratorAccess

Still can't go beyond the boundary.

Boundary = prevent privilege escalation when delegating IAM administration.

11. EventBridge also needs Permission

Rule match event is not enough.

EventBridge Rule
↓
Target

EventBridge must have permission to call target.

Example with Lambda:

EventBridge
↓
Lambda Resource Policy
↓
Allow InvokeFunction

For some targets such as ECS Task, EventBridge is often used:

IAM Role
↓
EventBridge assume role
↓
Run target

Rule match with event ≠ automatically has the right to invoke target.

12. Common IAM Global Condition Keys

Condition KeyMeaning of distinction
aws:PrincipalOrgIDCheck that the principal calling the request belongs to the correct AWS Organization ID (for example, limit the S3 bucket to the internal Organization without needing to list each Account ID)
aws:PrincipalOrgPathsThe principal limit must be within the Organizations specific OU path
aws:PrincipalTag/<Key>Compare tags attached to IAM User/Role or ABAC serving session
aws:SourceIpLimit the request's source IP/CIDR range (not applicable when calling through some intermediate AWS services that do not keep the original IP)
"Condition": { "StringEquals": { "aws:PrincipalOrgID": "o-abc123xyz" } }
Quick review · SAA-C03

Effective permissions are affected by Identity/Resource policies, SCP, Permission Boundary and Session Policy; Explicit Deny wins over Allow.

AWS IAM Identity Center

Purpose: AWS IAM Identity Center is used for single sign-on and centralized access management for multiple AWS accounts and business applications.

1 User
↓
1 Login / SSO
↓
Multiple AWS Accounts / Applications

Identity Center = central login + central access management.

1. Instead of creating IAM User in each Account

Shouldn't:

User Bob
├── IAM User in Dev
├── IAM User in Test
└── IAM User in Prod

With Identity Center:

Bob
↓
IAM Identity Center
↓
Dev / Test / Prod

→ Easier onboarding/offboarding management and avoiding permanent IAM User/access keys in each account.

2. Identity Source

Identity Center needs to know: Who is the user? · Which group?

Identity source can be: Identity Center built-in store · Active Directory · Okta · OneLogin · Other external IdP

For example:

Employee
↓
Okta
↓
IAM Identity Center
↓
AWS Accounts

3. Permission Set

Permission Set = set of permissions prepared for use in an AWS account.

ReadOnly · DeveloperAccess · DatabaseAdmin · FullAccess

Permission set can contain: AWS Managed Policies · Customer Managed Policies · Inline Policy

When assign permission is set to an account, Identity Center creates corresponding access rights through roles/sessions in that account.

4. Assignment

  • Assignment answers 3 questions: WHO? = User / Group
  • WHERE? = Which AWS Account?
  • WHAT? = Which Permission Set?
Developers Group
↓
Dev Account
↓
FullAccess
Developers Group
↓
Prod Account
↓
ReadOnlyAccess

Same group but permissions may be different for each account.

5. Login Flow

User logs into the portal:

User
↓
Identity Center / IdP
↓
SSO Portal
↓
Select AWS Account
↓
Select Permission Set
↓
Temporary Role Session
↓
AWS Console / CLI

Users do not need a separate password/IAM User in each AWS account.

6. Group in Identity Center ≠ IAM Group

Identity Center Group = user group to assign access to multiple accounts/apps

IAM Group = IAM Users group in an AWS account

Two different concepts.

7. SSO for Applications

Identity Center isn't just for AWS.

Can SSO to: Salesforce · Box · Microsoft 365 · SAML 2.0 Applications

Employee
↓
Identity Center
↓
AWS Account
or
Business Application

→ same identity source for multiple systems.

8. ABAC

Identity Center support Attribute-Based Access Control.

For example, the user has: Department = Data · CostCenter = 100

Resource has tag: Department = Data

Policy may stipulate:

Principal Department
=
Resource Department
↓
Allow

→ permissions can be changed based on attributes instead of creating many separate policies.

9. Practical example

Developer:

Login once
↓
Dev Account → FullAccess
Prod Account → ReadOnly

DBA:

costCenter = DB
↓
Permission Set
↓
Only manage resources with appropriate tags

Employee quits:

Disable/Delete identity in central source
↓
lost access to many AWS accounts/apps

→ do not have to delete each IAM User in each account.

10. Use Cases

Use cases:

Okta / AD
↓
Identity Center
↓
AWS Organizations Accounts
Developers
↓
Dev = FullAccess
Prod = ReadOnly
Identity Center
↓
Salesforce / Microsoft 365 / SAML Apps

11. Matrix Group × AWS Account × Permission Set

In IAM Identity Center, Group Determines which pairs the user sees and selects AWS Account + Permission Set Which right after SSO login:

GroupAWS AccountPermission Set received
DeveloperDEV AccountDeveloperAccess
DeveloperPROD AccountReadOnlyAccess (does not automatically have Admin rights)
AdministratorDEV / PROD AccountAdministratorAccess
Quick review · SAA-C03

IAM Identity Center provides SSO and permission sets to access multiple accounts; Actual permissions are often implemented via IAM Roles.

AWS Directory Service & Active Directory integration

Purpose: AWS Directory Service helps applications on AWS use Active Directory (AD): either create a directory on AWS, or connect to an existing company AD.

AD is where employee accounts, computers, and groups are managed. When Bob logs in, AD helps check Who is Bob?. Bob in AD different from IAM User in your AWS account.

1. Three options need to be distinguished

1.1. AWS Managed Microsoft AD

AWS creates and manages the infrastructure for one Microsoft AD runs on AWS. You can create users and groups in this AD for applications to use.

Bob is created in Microsoft AD on AWS
↓
Bob logs into the application
↓
AD on AWS authenticates Bob

Use when: Windows applications or machines on AWS need Microsoft AD.

  • If the company already has an AD in the office, it can be set up trust between two ADs.
  • Then a user like Bob can still reside in corporate AD and use that identity to access appropriate resources on AWS.

1.2. AD Connector

AD Connector is bridge From AWS to AD the company has it. It does not create a new AD to save the Bob account.

Bob is in the company AD
↓
Bob logs into the AWS service
↓
AD Connector forwards the request to corporate AD
↓
AD company authenticates Bob

Use when: The company already has AD and wants AWS services to use those users. Creating a user or changing Bob's password is still done in the company AD.

1.3. Simple AD

Simple AD is one basic, independent directory on AWS. You create and manage users in Simple AD.

Bob is created in Simple AD
↓
The application on AWS uses the Bob account

Use when: The workload needs basic directory functionality, but does not need full Microsoft AD or connection to corporate AD.

Simple AD Does not support trust with other AD and Do not use as an identity source for IAM Identity Center.

2. Quick comparison

ServiceWhere is Bob's account located?Main idea
AWS Managed Microsoft ADCan be in AD on AWS; You can also use users from trusted ADThere is Microsoft AD on AWS
AD ConnectorCompany AD already existsConnect AWS to existing AD
Simple ADSimple AD on AWSBasic, independent directory

3. Contact IAM Identity Center

If using AD as an identity source:

AD → determines who Bob is
IAM Identity Center → assign Bob permission to Dev / Test / Prod
  • Bob having an account in AD does not mean he can access an AWS account. Administrators must still assign permissions to Bob or Bob's group in IAM Identity Center.

4. Use cases

Use cases:

  • Migrate Windows/managed directory workloads to AWS but still establish two-way trust with corporate AD.
  • Use AD Connector when you want AWS to authenticate employees with on-prem AD without creating a directory copy in AWS.
  • Use Simple AD for standalone AWS applications that need directory AD-compatible but do not need on-premises federation.

5. Active Directory Forest Trust

Afternoon TrustAi tin ai?Access consequences
Outgoing TrustAWS Managed Microsoft AD tin domain On-premisesOn-premises users can access domain resources on AWS
Incoming TrustDomain On-premises tin AWS Managed Microsoft ADUsers belonging to AWS AD can access resources in the On-premises domain
Quick review · SAA-C03

Managed Microsoft AD, AD Connector and Simple AD address different directory needs; Select according to Windows/AD integration requirements.

AWS Control Tower

Purpose: AWS Control Tower helps set up and manage multiple AWS accounts under a common baseline. It uses AWS Organizations as its foundation, creating landing zones, allocating accounts, and tracking compliance with rules.

Remember: Control Tower is Construction and management tools; landing zone is created environment. Then, when the team needs a new account, the administrator can provision the account into this environment Account Factory.

AWS Organizations
↓
Control Tower builds landing zone
↓
Dev / Test / Prod accounts
↓
Apply controls + monitor compliance
  • Instead of each team creating its own account with its own configuration, administrators use Account Factory to level accounts in the Organization structure and apply baseline governance.

1. Guardrail: prevention is different from detective

Preventive control block violations before action is taken. Usage example SCP to restrict operations in unauthorized AWS Regions.

User calls API in restricted Region
↓
Preventive control check
↓
Deny

Detective control check configuration after the resource is created or changed, often passed AWS Config rule. For example: detecting a resource missing tags.

Resource is created but tag is missing
↓
AWS Config review
↓
NON_COMPLIANT
TypeThe mechanism in the exampleResults
PreventiveSCPBlock violations.
DetectiveAWS Config ruleDetect and report non-compliant resources.

Need to prevent creating wrong resources → preventive. Need to find the wrong resource → detective. Detecting a violation does not mean the action has been blocked or the resource has been fixed.

2. Notification and remediation flow

Suppose the team wants to find a resource that is missing a tag:

Resource in member account
↓
AWS Config rule evaluates the tag
↓
NON_COMPLIANT
↓
SNS notifies admin
↓
Lambda adds tags according to defined rules
↓
AWS Config reevaluated
  • SNS and Lambda are workflows that require additional configuration. Lambda needs permission to edit the resource and must know Which tag is correct?; Don't guess yourself CostCenter for resource production.
  • Preventive control operates in an earlier step: it Refuse unauthorized action, for example calling an API in a restricted Region, instead of waiting for AWS Config to detect it later.

3. Use cases

Administration situationHow to deal with Control Tower
Build a standard Landing ZoneCreate a multi-account environment with Organization structure, baseline governance and compliance dashboard
Limit AWS RegionsTurn on Preventive Control (SCP) to immediately block API calls in unauthorized Regions
Check & add TagUse AWS Config (Detective) to detect resources missing tags → report to SNS → run Lambda to tag according to clear rules

4. Three types of Control (Preventive / Proactive / Detective), Account Factory & Drift

Control typeTime of inspectionEnforcement mechanism
PreventiveImmediately upon calling the AWS API actionSCP/RCP blocks the offending operation
Proactive controlsBefore CloudFormation creates or updates resourcesCloudFormation Hooks check template/resource properties
DetectiveAfter the resource has been created or changedAWS Config Rules detect non-compliance status
Components & Drift in Control TowerFunction / Expression of deviation from the standard state
Account FactoryComponent that automatically allocates new AWS Accounts according to standard baselines (OU, network, logging CloudTrail/Config)
Account / OU DriftThe account is illegally transferred to an OU, deleted from the Organization or the Foundational OU is deleted
SCP / Control DriftThe SCP managed by the Control Tower is modified/removed or the control stops working as designed
Landing Zone / Inheritance DriftIAM Role required/trusted access is disabled or the account no longer inherits the correct baseline from the OU
Quick review · SAA-C03

Control Tower builds and manages landing zones according to guardrails/controls; Organizations takes care of the multi-account structure, Account Factory creates standard accounts.

LDAP, SAML 2.0, SCIM & Custom Identity Broker

Purpose: Differentiate the roles of each enterprise identity protocol and the two federation login flows to AWS.

1. Distinguishing table for LDAP, IdP, SAML 2.0, SCIM and STS

Composition/ProtocolCore functions
LDAPDirectory lookup protocol and username/password check directly against directory (e.g. Active Directory)
Identity Provider (IdP)System that authenticates users and issues federated login credentials (AD FS, Okta, Entra ID, Keycloak)
SAML 2.0Standard for transmitting authentication messages (SAML Assertion) from IdP to AWS for SSO login without revealing the password to AWS
SCIMProtocol Automatically synchronize Users and Groups lifecycle (create, edit, delete) from IdP to IAM Identity Center
Custom Identity BrokerThe middleware application builds itself when the internal system only has LDAP and There is no IdP that supports SAML/OIDC
AWS STS & Federation EndpointSTS grants temporary credentials; Federation Endpoint changes temporary credentials to sign-in URL into AWS Console

2. Two federated authentication flows need to be distinguished

Flow A — Without SAML (using Custom Identity Broker):
Employee → Custom Identity Broker → check LDAP → call AWS STS to get temporary credentials → call AWS Federation Endpoint → open AWS Console

Stream B — With SAML 2.0 Federation (preferred, no need to write Broker yourself):
Employee → IdP (authenticate with LDAP) → return SAML Assertion → send to AWS SAML Endpoint / STS (AssumeRoleWithSAML) → open AWS Console

EC2

Amazon EC2 fundamentals & launch configuration

Purpose: Run virtual servers when you need to control the operating system, software and how applications work.

1. What is EC2?

EC2 rents compute on demand. AWS manages the physical infrastructure; You manage the OS, patches, software and application data on your device.

An instance resides in an AZ. If you want to tolerate AZ errors, you need many instances across many AZs; A large machine does not automatically create High Availability.

2. Options when creating a machine

ComponentDecide what
AMIOriginal operating system and software
Instance typeCPU, RAM, network and I/O capabilities
VPC/subnetWhere is the device located and what network connection is there?
Security GroupTraffic is allowed in/out
IAM roleApplication permissions to call AWS services
StorageEBS for persistent disks; EFS for shared files; Instance Store for temporary data
Key pairUsed when logging in SSH; Can be administered using Systems Manager if configured properly

3. User Data

User Data is usually a script that installs a package, loads configuration, or starts an application.
On Linux, by default the script runs as root on first boot; can structure
image reruns.

Do not put secrets directly in User Data. When needed, the application retrieves secrets from the Secrets Manager/Parameter Store using a role with limited permissions.

4. Compile into a scalable application

Users → ALB → EC2 in Auto Scaling Group in multiple AZs
                   └─ Persistent data in appropriate database/storage

Launch Template saves instance creation configuration so ASG can recreate machines consistently. CloudWatch monitors load; ASG adjusts engine numbers.

5. CloudWatch monitoring

EC2 monitoring modeCollection Cycle & Range
Basic MonitoringDefault free: most EC2 metrics sent each 5 minutes (Status check metrics are still available every 1 minute)
Detailed MonitoringPay extra to receive EC2 host metrics every time 1 minute; no automatically collect RAM or disk inside the OS (AWS Docs)
CloudWatch AgentInstall inside the OS to push RAM, swap, disk space and application logs to CloudWatch

6. Use cases

Use cases: Web/API needs its own package; Legacy applications need OS control; batch or HPC needs specific type of CPU/GPU. Use tags to manage and view costs by environment.

7. AWS Local Zones, EC2 Swap Space & Apache Tomcat

Components/ServicesNature & Architectural Notes
AWS Local ZonesExtension of one parent Region Place compute/storage near major urban centers to achieve latency single-digit milliseconds; Physical location separate from main AZ so need to check data residency requirements
EC2 Swap SpaceArea on disk used temporarily by OS when RAM is full (slower than RAM); Basic/Detailed Monitoring is not collected by itself but must be used CloudWatch Agent (swap_used, swap_free, swap_used_percent, mem_used_percent)
Apache TomcatJava Servlet/JSP web container running on EC2, Beanstalk or containers (persistent server/container model, distinct from AWS Lambda serverless functions)
Quick review · SAA-C03

EC2 is located in an AZ; If you want high availability, you need many machines across many AZs. User Data takes care of initialization, IAM Role takes care of AWS API access.

EC2 instance types, lifecycle & Hibernate

Purpose: Choose the machine configuration and shutdown method appropriate to resources, startup time and data to keep.

1. Select instance family

FamilySuitable
T/M – general purposeWeb, dev/test; T instances use CPU credits for burst workloads
C – compute optimizedHeavy processing of CPU, encoding, batch
R/X – memory optimizedIn-memory database, large dataset in RAM
I/D – storage optimizedLocal I/O or large data on disk
P/G – accelerated computingGPU, ML or graphics

The name m5.2xlarge includes family M, generation 5 and size 2xlarge.

No need to memorize the vCPU table of each machine; Select by CPU, RAM, I/O or network bottleneck.

2. Reboot, Stop and Terminate

OperationWhat happens?
RebootRestart the OS; holds instance and storage.
StopStop EBS-backed instance: keep EBS, lose RAM and Instance Store. Self-provisioned Public IPv4 usually changes when restarting.
TerminateDelete instance. EBS with DeleteOnTermination will be deleted; Volumes that do not enable this attribute remain and continue to charge.

Note: Stop does not free EBS.

3. Hibernate

Hibernate writes RAM to the encrypted EBS root volume and then stops the computer.

When starting, RAM and processes are reloaded, which means the application takes a long time to prepare data in memory.

RAM → EBS root encrypted → stop → start → reload RAM

Must enable hibernation support when launching, use a supported device/AMI, and root with enough RAM space.

Do not keep hibernate for more than 60 days; Instance Store still loses data. Details limited by OS.

4. Use cases

Use cases: C-family for video encoding; R-family for data processing in RAM. Hibernate for dev environments or slow application startups; Use Multi-AZ and backup to protect availability/data.

Quick review · SAA-C03

Reboot keeps the instance; Stop keeps EBS but loses RAM; Terminate deletes the instance according to disk configuration. Hibernate saves RAM to encrypted EBS to restore state.

EC2 networking, Security Groups & IAM

Purpose: Control your application's network connectivity and access to AWS on EC2.

1. Security Group

Security Group is a stateful firewall attached to ENI.
There is only Allow rule; The rules of multiple SGs are combined.
The new SG defaults to no inbound and no outbound; The response of the connection is
self-passage.
Internet → ALB SG: HTTPS 443
ALB → App SG: indicates the source is ALB SG
App → DB SG: indicates the source is App SG on the database port

SG reference helps avoid having to update IP when ASG changes machines.

Timeout can be due to SG, NACL or route; connection refused is usually because there is no listening service on that port.

2. Public and private IP

Private IP used in VPC network.

EC2 direct Internet access needs public IP with route to Internet Gateway and appropriate network rules; Just being in the public subnet is not enough.

Private instances can go to the Internet via NAT or call AWS services via VPC
Endpoint.
Commonly used SSH 22, HTTPS 443, RDP 3389; Should limit administrative sources or use
Systems Manager.

3. ENI and Elastic IP

ENI: Virtual network card contains private IP, MAC and SG, belonging to one AZ.

Secondary ENI can switch between instances in the same AZ; primary ENI is not removed from the instance in use.

Elastic IP is a fixed public IPv4 that can be reattached to another resource.
Public IPv4 has a fee; Prioritize ALB/DNS if fixed IP is not needed.
The default EIP Quota is usually 5 per Region, can be increased.

4. Enhanced Networking and EFA

Elastic Fabric Adapter (EFA) is a network interface for EC2 running HPC or distributed ML, helping instances exchange data with low latency and high throughput.

MechanismFunction
Enhanced Networking / ENASR-IOV helps increase bandwidth/PPS and reduce latency for conventional IP networks. ENA is the modern choice; Intel 82599 VF is legacy.
EFAAdd OS-bypass via Libfabric for MPI/NCCL compatible applications; tightly coupled workloads require continuous data exchange.

Needs instance type, OS, driver/library and Security Group supported by EFA. Used for multi-node HPC simulation or distributed ML training.

5. IAM role for EC2

Instance profile brings the IAM role into EC2.
SDK retrieves temporary credentials via metadata service; Enable IMDSv2 and grant dark permissions
minimal.
Do not save personal access keys on the device.

Network permission and IAM permission are two separate layers: opening a port does not grant read access to S3; Role level does not create network routes.

6. Use cases

Use cases: EC2 private reads S3 by role and S3 VPC Endpoint. ALB receives HTTPS from users, EC2 only receives requests from ALB SG, database only receives requests from app SG.

7. Detailed distinction between ENA vs EFA (Networking & HPC)

CriteriaENA (Elastic Network Adapter)EFA (Elastic Fabric Adapter)
Main purposeEnhanced Networking standard for EC2 (high bandwidth, large PPS)HPC & Distributed AI/ML multi-node tightly coupled
Transmission mechanismGoes through the operating system's TCP/IP stackSupport OS-bypass qua Libfabric and communication MPI to minimize latency
Quick review · SAA-C03

Security Group is the stateful firewall for ENI; IAM Role grants AWS API permissions, not routes or network rules. Fast exchange multi-node HPC → consider EFA.

EC2 purchasing & placement strategies

Purpose: Reduce costs and choose the right machine placement for latency, fault tolerance or licensing requirements.

Choose to buy by workload:

On-Demand = flexible
Savings Plans / Reserved Instances = commitment to discounts
Spot = low prices, subject to disruption
Capacity Reservation = capacity reservation

1. Choose how to pay

ChooseWhen used
On-DemandUnpredictable loading, short-term, unwilling to commit
Reserved InstancesStable loading, 1 or 3 year commitment; Standard/Convertible have different levels of flexibility
Savings PlansCommit to spending per hour for 1 or 3 years
SpotLoad is tolerant of interruptions, has retry/checkpoint
Capacity ReservationNeed to keep capacity at one AZ, no discount
Dedicated InstanceNeed hardware isolated from other accounts
Dedicated HostNeed to control physical host or license by socket/core

Compute Savings Plans are flexible across family/Region and Fargate/Lambda; EC2 Instance Savings Plans are tied to family and Region, flexible in size/OS/tenancy.

Regional RI and Zonal RI

Scope of RIDiscountKeep capacity
Regional RIApplicable in Region; Has size flexibility for Linux/UNIX, default tenancy when appropriateNo
Zonal RIApplies to matching EC2 configurations in the selected AZ; no AZ/instance-size flexibilityYes, for specific configurations within an AZ

Standard/Convertible is the offering class; Regional/Zonal is scope, two different classifications.

2. Spot needs an interruption-tolerant design

Spot uses available capacity and can be reclaimed by AWS.

Interruption announcements are usually made about 2 minutes in advance; do not consider it a guaranteed time for any interruption.

Use multiple instance types/AZ, checkpoints and external persistent data storage
instance. instance.
Consistent batch, CI and worker reruns; poor job fit impossible
interruption.

3. Keeping capacity is different from discounting

Capacity Reservation guarantees capacity in the selected AZ; still pay for the portion kept even if not used.

Matching discounts from RI/Savings Plans may be combined; Regional RI/Savings Plans do not hold their own capacity; Zonal RI holds capacity for matching configurations in the selected AZ.

4. Placement Groups

StrategyHow to set up the machineTrade-off
ClusterClose together in an AZLow Latency, high throughput; does not accept AZ's fault
SpreadSeparate hardware for each machine, possibly multiple AZsIsolate small groups of important machines; Up to 7 running instances/AZ with rack-level spread
PartitionSplit a rack group into partitionsKafka/HDFS/Cassandra fusion; Maximum 7 partitions/AZ

5. Use cases

Use cases: HPC needs very fast exchange nodes: Cluster. Important nodes that need to be isolated for hardware errors: Spread. Large distributed system self-replicates data: Partition. Batch can be run again: Spot combined with checkpoint.

6. Distinguish between Regional RI vs Zonal RI & Offering Class

RI Properties (1 or 3 years)Core distinction
Regional RI vs Zonal RIRegional RI flexible AZ and instance size in the same family but does not hold capacity; Zonal RI attaches a specific AZ and There is a capacity reservation
Standard RI vs Convertible RIStandard offers deeper discounts but limits exchange of instance families; Convertible reduces less but changes to another instance family/OS/tenancy of equal or higher value
Quick review · SAA-C03

Spot is economical but can be disruptive; Savings Plans/Regional RI reduces prices but does not maintain capacity. Cluster/Spread/Partition are three different ways of distributing instances.

EC2 Storage Options

Purpose: Select persistent disk, temporary disk or shared file for EC2.

1. Compare storage

StorageMain characteristics
EBSBlock storage over the network, in the same AZ as EC2. Keep data when stopped; terminate delete or keep depending on DeleteOnTermination, the default root is usually deleted.
Instance StoreDisk local, fast I/O. Data loss when stopping/hibernate/terminate or host failure; reboot still holds. Contains only reproducible data.
EFSManaged NFS/POSIX for Linux, multiple EC2 mounts. Regional EFS is shared across many AZs, capacity expands automatically.

2. EBS volume types

TypeSuitable
gp3 / gp2 — SSDGeneral purpose. gp3: baseline 3,000 IOPS, 125 MiB/s; IOPS/throughput are capacity independent. gp2: IOPS increases with size, has burst credits.
io1 / io2 Block Express — SSDDatabase needs high IOPS, stability and low latency.
st1 — HDDBig data, warehouse, log; throughput for regular sequential I/O.
sc1 — HDDLow-access data, low cost priority.

Boot volume used SSD. EBS charges for provisioned capacity, even when EC2 is stopped.

EBS featureConditions & Notes
Multi-AttachSupport only io1/io2, maximum 16 EC2 Nitro in same AZ; Required to use application and cluster-aware file system (not using regular EXT4/XFS for multiple machines to record at the same time)
EncryptionKMS protects volumes, EC2–EBS traffic and snapshots; Do not enable it directly on the old unencrypted volume, but must create a new volume from the snapshot

3. Snapshots and AMIs

Snapshot = backup EBS at a time, save the changed block next time
Convert AZ = snapshot → create volume at destination AZ → attach EC2
AMI = OS/software/configuration model to launch EC2; belongs to Region, can be copied

Snapshots do not need detach, but need flush/pause writes for consistency. Archive: restore 24–72 hours; Recycle Bin: keeps snapshots deleted by mistake for 1 day–1 year; FSR: avoids slowness when reading the block for the first time, has separate fees.

4. EFS

EC2 Linux → mount target in AZ → shared EFS. The SG of the mount target is open TCP 2049 from SG EC2; Encrypt at rest with KMS, in transit with TLS.

Regional = multiple AZs; One Zone = one AZ, lower cost
General Purpose = low latency
Elastic = self-adjusting throughput; Provisioned = reserved
Bursting = throughput according to Standard capacity and credits
Standard = regular access; IA/Archive = less traffic
Lifecycle policies = automatically change class according to inactive time

IA/Archive has an access fee; Archive requires Regional + Elastic throughput. One Zone supports Standard/IA.

5. Use cases

Use cases: EBS gp3 for boot/app; io2 for databases; Instance Store for cache/scratch; Regional EFS for shared WordPress upload directory between multiple EC2s.

Details: trang EBS.

Quick review · SAA-C03

EBS = persistent block disk in an AZ; EFS = shared file system; Instance Store = temporary local disk. Choose according to durability and sharing needs.

EBS & EFS

Amazon EBS: persistent block storage

Purpose: Provide durable disk for EC2, used for operating system, filesystem and database.

Usage flow:

EC2
↓ attach block volume in the same AZ
EBS
↓
Filesystem/Database

1. Volume belongs to one AZ

EBS: block storage over the network.

Volume and EC2 must be in the same AZ; Usually a volume is attached to one instance, an instance can have many volumes.

Volume remains when the device is stopped.

When terminating, DeleteOnTermination decides whether to delete the volume; root is usually on, data volume is usually off.

Check the property instead of assuming that the data is always present.

2. Select volume type

TypeSuitable
gp3Universal SSD; Configure capacity, IOPS, and throughput separately
gp2Universal SSD; Baseline IOPS depends on capacity, small volumes have bursts
io1/io2Provisioned IOPS for stable I/O; io2 for databases needs low latency and high durability
st1HDD for large data, frequent sequential read/write
sc1HDD for rarely accessed data, prioritizing cost
SSD can be used as a boot volume; st1/sc1 is not used as a boot volume.
IOPS is the number of operations per second; throughput is the amount of data per second.
Database random I/O is different from processing large files sequentially.

3. EBS specifications

Limit according to AWS documentation check date 13/10/2026; Actual speed depends on EC2 instance, I/O size and volume configuration.

TypeCapacityMax IOPSMax throughput
gp21 GiB–16 TiB16.000250 MiB/s
gp31 GiB–64 TiB80.0002.000 MiB/s
io14 GiB–16 TiB64.0001.000 MiB/s
io2 Block Express4 GiB–64 TiB256.0004.000 MiB/s
st1125 GiB–16 TiB500500 MiB/s
sc1125 GiB–16 TiB250250 MiB/s

High level requires EC2/Nitro support and sufficient EBS bandwidth; gp2 throughput depends on capacity. gp3 on Outposts has lower limits: 16 TiB, 16,000 IOPS, 1,000 MiB/s.

4. gp3 and volume expansion

Baseline gp3: 3,000 IOPS and 125 MiB/s.

You can buy more performance without having to increase disk space just to get IOPS.

The current gp3 limit has increased compared to the old slide; see AWS EBS gp3 during actual configuration.

Elastic Volumes allows increasing capacity or changing performance/type while the machine is running; may need to expand partition/filesystem in OS.

EBS charges based on the capacity allocated, not just the number of bytes in use.

5. Multi-Attach

io1/io2 supports mounting the same volume to up to 16 Nitro instances in the same AZ when the configuration is supported.

Machines can read/write, but the application must have locking and filesystem cluster-aware to avoid data corruption.

Multi-Attach does not turn EBS into a multi-AZ shared file system. Multiple Linux servers sharing the same files are often more suitable for EFS.

6. Use cases

Use cases: Boot disk/dev server: gp3. Database on EC2 needs consistent I/O: io2. Log or sequential big data: st1. Want to transfer AZ: snapshot and then create a new volume in the destination AZ.

7. Max IOPS/GiB & EBS RAID Ratio (RAID 0 vs RAID 1)

EBS typeMax IOPS / GiBMax IOPS / VolumeMax Throughput
gp3500 IOPS/GiB (above baseline 3,000 IOPS)80.0002.000 MiB/s
io150 IOPS/GiB64.0001.000 MiB/s
io2 Block Express1.000 IOPS/GiB256.0004.000 MiB/s
CriteriaRAID 0 (Striping)RAID 1 (Mirroring)
Mechanism & PurposeBanding data across multiple EBSs to aggregate IOPS and throughputRecord identical replication on 2 drives to increase drive redundancy
AWS Risks & RecommendationsOne volume error causes data loss in the entire RAID 0 array; Used when a single volume does not have enough bandwidth/IOPSLess necessary on EBS because each EBS volume is self-replicated on many devices in the same AZ (avoid RAID 5/6 due to I/O parity costs)
Quick review · SAA-C03

gp3 for popular SSD needs; io1/io2 for stable IOPS; st1/sc1 for sequential throughput or infrequently accessed data. EBS still charges when EC2 Stops.

EBS snapshots, AMI & encryption

Purpose: Backup disk, create a consistent machine, and protect EBS data with encryption.

1. Snapshot

A snapshot is a copy at a point in time.
The first snapshot of the block copy contains data; Next time save the changed block.
Deleting old snapshots does not eliminate the ability to restore from remaining snapshots.

It is not required to remove the volume for snapshots. If you want to backup consistently with the database, you need to flush/quiesce the appropriate application or backup process. Snapshots do not contain RAM.

Volume can be created in another AZ from snapshot.

Copying snapshot to another Region/account requires appropriate sharing and KMS permissions; snapshots using AWS managed keys are not shared cross-account like customer managed keys.

2. Backup features

FeaturesSolve the problem
Snapshot ArchiveSave rarely used backups at a lower cost; Restore usually takes 24–72 hours
Recycle BinRecover accidentally deleted snapshots/AMIs if retention rules are in place
Fast Snapshot RestoreVolumes created from snapshots have full performance immediately, avoiding first-read latency; There is an additional fee according to AZ

EBS Snapshot Archive

EBS Snapshot Archive is a lower-cost storage class for snapshots that rarely need to be restored, such as long-term backups.

CriteriaSnapshot StandardSnapshot Archive
PurposeBackup regularly, need to restore soonLong-term backup, rarely recover
Storage unit priceHigherLower; There is an additional restoration fee
How to saveIncremental: saves blocks changed after the first snapshotFull snapshot: the entire block has data at the time of the snapshot
Create volumesCan be used directly to create volumeMust restore to Standard first, may take up to 72 hours
Minimum durationThere is no minimum retention periodCharge for a minimum of 90 days

If you permanently delete or restore to Standard before 90 days, you will still have to pay the Archive fee for the remaining time. Because Archive saves full snapshots, the billed capacity may be larger than the incremental version; Need to consider both capacity and restore fee.

3. AMI

AMI: EC2 creation sample: OS, software, configuration and disk information.

With EBS-backed AMI, disk data resides in EBS snapshots.

Install and configure EC2 → create AMI → Launch Template → ASG create the same machine

AMI has a Region scope; Copy to another Region before using there. Update AMI and patches periodically, do not leave secrets in the image.

4. Amazon Data Lifecycle Manager (DLM)

Amazon DLM automatically create, retain and delete EBS snapshots / EBS-backed AMIs according to policy; No need to write your own Lambda or cron for a simple backup schedule.

EC2 + EBS volumes
↓ DLM policy: backup every day
EBS snapshots
↓ keep 7 days
Automatically delete expired snapshots
FunctionMeaning
ScheduleCreate scheduled snapshots or EBS-backed AMIs; Select resources by policy/tags
RetentionKeep by number of copies or time, depending on policy
DeleteDelete expired snapshots; deregister AMI and process related snapshots according to policy
CopyCopy snapshot to another Region according to support policy

DLM manages copies created by DLM itself; Do not self-manage snapshots/AMIs created in other ways and do not use Instance Store-backed AMIs. Choose AWS Backup when you need centralized policies for multiple services.

5. EBS encryption

KMS protects volumes, snapshots, and data transfers between EC2/EBS.
Snapshots from encrypted volumes are also encrypted; volume created from encrypted
snapshots hold encryption.

Do not turn on encryption directly on the old unencrypted volume: snapshot → copy encrypted snapshot → create a new encrypted volume → replace volume.

The role needs KMS permission to use the key.

6. Use cases

Use cases: Golden AMI for ASG; cross-Region snapshot/copy for DR; Recycle Bin prevents accidental deletion; Convert legacy disk to encrypted volume using snapshot.

7. EBS Encryption by Default, Snapshot Archive & Snapshot Lock

Mechanism on EBSFunctions & Rules to remember
EBS Encryption by DefaultSet at level Account for each Region so that all newly created EBS volumes and snapshots in that Region are automatically encrypted (use alias/aws/ebs or CMK)
EBS Snapshot ArchiveCheap full snapshot storage tier (min 90 days); If you want to create a volume, you must restore it to Standard first (it takes up to 72 hours). Snapshots stored on S3 are managed by AWS, so they are not visible in your S3 Console
EBS Snapshot LockLock to prevent deletion of snapshots by time in mode Governance (only people with special IAM permissions can remove it) or Compliance (absolutely immutable until expiration, including root)
Quick review · SAA-C03

Snapshot is a backup disk; The AMI is an EC2 launch template. DLM self-manages lifecycle snapshots/AMIs created by DLM itself; encryption and cross-account copy require appropriate KMS permissions.

EBS vs EFS vs Instance Store

Purpose: Distinguish between private disks, shared files and temporary disks to avoid choosing the wrong place to save data.

Three ways to save data:

EBS = block volume for EC2
EFS = shared file system
Instance Store = temporary disk on the host

1. Key comparison

CriteriaEBSEFSInstance Store
TypeBlock disk over the networkShared file system NFS/POSIXLocal block disk on the host
ShareUsually an EC2; Conditional Multi-AttachMultiple Linux clients mounted togetherAttach to instance/host
ScopeOne AZMulti-AZ Regional or One ZoneA host
After stop/host errorEBS retains data, except for configuration deletionIndependent of EC2All data may be lost
Used forBoot disk, databaseShared content, home directory, containersCache, buffer, scratch

2. How does EFS work?

Create a mount target in AZ, enable NFS 2049 via SG and then mount from the Linux client. EFS automatically increases/decreases capacity according to the file, without needing to allocate volume size first.

Regional EFS saves across multiple AZs for better fault tolerance.

One Zone is cheaper but doesn't want to lose the whole AZ.

Encrypt at rest using KMS; You can turn on encryption in transit and use access points to limit paths/permissions.

3. Performance and EFS costs

General Purpose is suitable for most latency-sensitive applications. Max I/O is the choice for some very large parallel workloads, with higher latency.

Elastic throughput adjusts itself according to load; Provisioned sets its own throughput level; Bursting ties throughput capacity to saved data and burst credits.

Lifecycle can transfer files to IA/Archive to reduce storage fees, in exchange for access fees and class conditions.

4. Instance Store

Very fast because the disk is near the compute.
Data may remain after reboot but is lost when stopped, hibernate, terminate or otherwise
hardware error.
Do not save single copies of important data; Use backup/replication or
other data sources.

5. Use cases

Use cases: WordPress or multiple ECS tasks need to see the same file: EFS. Private database on an EC2: EBS. Handles reproducible intermediate data: Instance Store. Windows SMB share: see FSx for Windows File Server.

6. EFS Storage Classes, Throughput Modes & Performance Modes

EFS Storage ClassAccess characteristicsAZ range
EFS StandardFrequent access, low latencyMulti-AZ
EFS Standard-IALess access, reduced storage fees (with retrieval fee)Multi-AZ
EFS ArchiveRare data access, long-term storage with lowest cost on Multi-AZMulti-AZ
EFS One Zone / One Zone-IASave money on dev/build or renewable dataSingle-AZ

EFS Intelligent-Tiering Use Lifecycle Management to automatically transfer files to IA/Archive according to inactive periods and then return them to Standard when read again.

EFS configuration groupThe modes need to be distinguished
Throughput ModeElastic (self-scaling for fluctuating load, needs General Purpose) · Provisioned (capacity independent MiB/s reservation) · Bursting (scaled according to storage capacity + burst credits)
Performance ModeGeneral Purpose (lowest latency per operation, default recommendation) · Max I/O (very high parallelism but higher latency; does not support Elastic and One Zone)
Quick review · SAA-C03

Multiple Linux clients share files → EFS; boot/database disk → EBS; renewable cache/scratch → Instance Store.

ALB, ASG & Beanstalk

High availability & Elastic Load Balancing

Purpose: Split requests to multiple machines and keep the application running when one machine or AZ fails.

1. Scalability and High Availability

Vertical scaling is changing to a stronger/weaker device. Horizontal scaling is adding/removing machines. High Availability (HA) means having spare resources and a mechanism to switch to healthy resources.

A large machine increases capacity but is still the single point of failure. With web apps, often use load balancer and ASG across multiple AZs.

2. What does Load Balancer do?

The load balancer receives the connection from the client and then transfers it to the downstream target.

ELB: managed service: AWS operates the load balancer infrastructure, you configure listener, target and health check.

Client → Route 53 → Load Balancer
                    ├─ healthy target in AZ A
                    └─ healthy target in AZ B

Internet-facing load balancer receives traffic from the Internet; internal load balancer serves connection in private network.

Clients often use DNS names, not fixed ALB IPs.

3. Check health and remaining capacity

Health check helps avoid sending traffic to an error target during normal operation.

When one AZ is lost, the remaining AZ must have enough capacity; ELB does not automatically create additional EC2 without ASG/scaling mechanism.

Databases, files, and sessions also need fault-tolerant design. Putting the session to an external store helps users continue working when the request is transferred to another machine.

4. Use cases

Use cases: Web/API has many EC2/ECS tasks: ELB shares load, ASG manages number of machines. Microservice private: internal ALB/NLB. Use Route 53 Alias ​​to point the domain to ELB, ACM for TLS and CloudWatch for monitoring.

Quick review · SAA-C03

Vertical scaling increases the power of each machine; horizontal scaling more machines; HA needs to distribute error domains and transfer traffic to healthy targets.

ALB vs NLB vs GWLB

Purpose: Choose load balancer according to protocol and how traffic needs to be handled.

1. Simply understand the 4 types of Load Balancers

Type

Understand simply

When to choose?

ALB

Delivered according to HTTP request content

Website, API, Microservices

NLB

Distributed by network connection, supporting static IP

TCP/UDP, game, IP allowlist

GWLB

Pass traffic through the security checking system

Firewall, IDS/IPS

CLB

Old Load Balancer

Legacy applications

2. ALB — Application Load Balancer (Layer 7)

ALB understands the HTTP request content so it can route by URL, hostname, header...

For example:

Client
   ↓
ALB (HTTPS)
   │
   ├── /users  → EC2 User Service
   │
   ├── /orders → EC2 Order Service
   │
   └── /images → Lambda

An ALB can serve multiple microservices through Target Groups.

3. NLB — Network Load Balancer (Layer 4)

NLB distributes network connections, not URL parsing like ALB.

Game Clients
      ↓
 NLB (UDP/TCP)
      │
      ├── Game Server A
      ├── Game Server B
      └── Game Server C

Special:

  • Supports static IP according to AZ.

  • Elastic IP can be assigned to Internet-facing NLB.

  • Suitable for TCP, UDP, TLS protocols and workloads that require low latency.

  • NLB → ALB can be set when both static IP and HTTP routing are needed.

4. GWLB — Gateway Load Balancer (Layer 3)

GWLB does not primarily deliver HTTP requests to the application, but rather sends traffic through security appliances.

Internet
    ↓
Gateway Load Balancer
    ↓
Firewall / IDS / IPS
    │
    ├── Appliance A
    └── Appliance B
    ↓
Application

For example: The company wants all traffic to be inspected by a third-party firewall before reaching EC2.

GWLB uses GENEVE, UDP port 6081 to transfer traffic to appliances

Quick review · SAA-C03

ALB = Layer 7 / HTTP routing; NLB = Layer 4 / TCP-UDP and static IP; GWLB = send traffic through security appliances; Club = legacy.

ELB targets, health, sessions & TLS

Purpose: Understand how load balancers choose targets, protect connections, and handle requests when deploying or scaling-in.

Request flow:

Client
↓ TLS / Listener
Load Balancer
↓ Rule → Target Group
Healthy Target

1. Listener, rule and target group

Listener receives traffic at protocol/port, for example HTTPS 443. Rule selects target group; target group contains backends and health check configuration.

Health check checks port/path such as /health, according to set interval and number of success/failures. Matcher can configure successful HTTP codes, not always just 200. Endpoints should reflect the service capabilities of the application.

2. Security Group and IP client

Let the client enter the ALB SG on the necessary port; SG app shows ALB SG power on backend/health check port. No need to open the entire backend to the Internet.

With ALB, the application gets the client IP from X-Forwarded-For instead of just looking at the ALB's IP connection.

NLB has a client IP preservation mechanism depending on target/protocol; Network security must still be configured correctly.

3. Sticky session

ALB stickiness uses cookies to keep a client on a target: cookies created by LB such as AWSALB or application-based cookies.

NLB stickiness uses source IP when supported, not HTTP cookies.

Stickiness can distort the load and not protect the session when the target dies.

Saving sessions in ElastiCache/DynamoDB helps machines share the same state. ALB sticky sessions.

4. Cross-zone load balancing

Enable cross-zone: LB node can send traffic to targets in other AZs.
Off: node only sends to targets in its AZ; Different numbers of machines can do it
Load each machine unevenly.
ALB turns on at load balancer level, can be adjusted at target group.
NLB/GWLB defaults to off; Transfer fee behavior varies by LB type, so check
Check when deployed.

5. TLS and SNI

ACM provides/manages certificates for HTTPS/TLS listeners.

SNI lets the client send its hostname in the TLS handshake so ALB/NLB can select the correct certificate; CLB does not support multiple certificates through SNI.

TLS client → LB and TLS LB → backend are two separate hops. To encrypt across hops, configure both the listener and the backend protocol accordingly.

6. Deregistration delay and Use cases

Use cases: When the target is deregistered, the LB stops sending new requests to it and gives the running request time to complete. This is deregistration delay; The default time is usually 300 seconds, need to adjust according to request length.

Used for deployment or ASG scale-in to avoid cutting requests.
Combine graceful shutdown of the app; Don't consider delay as a way to save every request
on target is broken.
Quick review · SAA-C03

Health check determines healthy target; sticky session and TLS termination address different needs. Distinguish client–LB connection from LB–target connection.

Auto Scaling Groups & Launch Templates

Purpose: Automatically maintain the required number of EC2 instances and recreate instances with a consistent configuration.

1. Minimum, desired and maximum

Minimum is the minimum number of machines; desired is the number of machines ASG is trying to maintain; maximum is
highest limit.
If a machine is terminated or determined to be unhealthy, ASG creates a replacement machine
about desired.

Select subnet in multiple AZs to distribute machines. ASG does not protect data that is only located on the device from being deleted, so the application should be stateless or use external storage.

2. Launch Template

Launch Template saves AMI, instance type, SG, IAM instance profile, User Data, key pair and disk. ASG selects subnet/AZ and attaches the target group according to the group's configuration.

Template has version.

Changing the template does not automatically make every old device a new device; Use Instance Refresh to replace the fleet to a new configuration with capacity control.

3. ASG and ELB

Launch Template → ASG creates EC2 → registers target group → ELB sends request
                       ↑
          health check / scaling policy

ASG uses EC2 health check and can turn on ELB health check to replace devices that the app cannot serve. Need health check grace period to not delete bootstrap device.

4. Controlled launch and termination

Lifecycle hook pauses at a launch/terminate step to install, complete work, or drain the connection.

Mixed Instances Policy combines multiple instance types with On-Demand/Spot, helping to get flexible capacity and reduce costs.

5. Use cases

Use cases: Web fleet multiple AZs after ALB; SQS processing worker can create/delete according to backlog; Patch rollout using new AMI and Instance Refresh.

Quick review · SAA-C03

ASG maintains the required number of EC2s across multiple AZs; Launch Template defines the machines to be launched, while scaling policy decides when to add/remove machines.

ASG scaling policies, metrics & cooldowns

Purpose: Increase capacity when lacking capacity and reduce capacity when excess, based on signals reflecting application load.

Stream scales according to load:

Metric / Alarm
↓ Scaling Policy
Auto Scaling Group
↓ increase or decrease desired capacity
EC2 instances

1. Scaling types

PolicyHow it worksFor example
Target trackingKeep metrics close to targetAverage CPU is about 50%
Step scalingThe more you exceed the threshold, the stronger the increaseCPU 70–85% add 1; above 85% add 3
Simple scalingAn alarm triggers a change, then waits for a cooldownHigh CPU → add 2 machines
ScheduledChange capacity according to known scheduleAdd machines before peak hours
PredictiveForecast from historical patterns to prepare in advanceThe load increases repeatedly every morning

2. Select metrics

CPU is suitable when the application is limited by CPU. ALBRequestCountPerTarget is suitable for web when requests are relatively similar. Network metrics for workloads with bandwidth congestion.

SQS workers should use backlogs per instance, not just total messages: 1,000 messages with 2 workers is different from 1,000 messages with 100 workers.

Metric target tracking should reflect usage per unit of capacity.

3. Warmup, grace period and cooldown

Warmup is the time a new instance takes to stabilize before being properly factored into a scaling decision. Health check grace period avoid hitting unhealthy too soon after launch.

Cooldowns are mainly used for simple scaling, avoiding continuous reactions before the previous effect is visible.

Target tracking/step scaling using warmup; There is no general rule that every scaling is locked for 300 seconds.

4. Increase quickly, decrease cautiously

Creating an AMI with available software helps the machine to serve soon.
Let the running request/job complete before scale-in; Use deregistration
delay/lifecycle hook when needed.
Always set maximum for the required load and monitor quota/capacity.

5. Use cases

Use cases: Web volatility: target tracking. Campaign knows start time: scheduled. SQS backlog grows very fast: step scaling or target tracking on backlog per worker.

Quick review · SAA-C03

Target tracking keeps metrics close to target; step scaling by threshold; scheduled scaling proactively by the hour. Choose according to loading pattern and boot time.

AWS Elastic Beanstalk

Purpose: Elastic Beanstalk helps deploy web applications to AWS quickly without having to manually build each part such as EC2, Auto Scaling, Load Balancer and monitoring.

Code
↓
Elastic Beanstalk
↓
EC2 + ASG + Load Balancer

Elastic Beanstalk = PaaS, not serverless.

1. How it works

Developers mainly worry about: Application Code

Beanstalk supports: Provision infrastructure · Deploy application · Auto Scaling · Load Balancing · Health Monitoring

Support: Java · .NET · Node.js · Python · Go · PHP · Docker

You can still access and configure the resources below.

2. Web Server Tier

Used for web/API to receive HTTP requests.

Client
↓
Load Balancer
↓
EC2 in Auto Scaling Group

Suitable: Website · REST API · Synchronous Request

3. Worker Tier

Used for background jobs.

Web App
↓
SQS
↓
Worker Environment
↓
Process Job

For example:

Send Email · Resize Image · Generate Report

Web Tier = HTTP Worker Tier = SQS + async job

4. Deployment

  • Important deployment types: Rolling = update each group of instances
  • Immutable = create a new instance and replace the old instance
  • Traffic Splitting = divide traffic between old and new versions

5. Database

Production database should be separate from the Beanstalk lifecycle:

Elastic Beanstalk
↓
Application
↓
Private RDS

→ Terminating the environment does not destroy the production database.

6. Cost

Elastic Beanstalk itself has no separate fees.

You pay for the resources below: EC2 · Load Balancer · RDS · S3 · ...

7. AWS App Runner

AWS App Runner Run web applications or APIs from source code or container images without needing to manage EC2, ECS or Kubernetes yourself. Services take care of infrastructure, deployment, load balancing, HTTPS endpoint and automatic scaling.

Java Spring Boot REST API
↓
Source code / Container image
↓
AWS App Runner
↓ self-deploy and self-scale
HTTPS Endpoint
↓
Users calls GET /products

For example, package the Spring Boot backend into a container image on ECR, deploy with App Runner for users to call GET /products. App Runner matches the receiving application HTTP requests; Scheduled batch jobs should use a dispatch service like AWS Batch combined with EventBridge Scheduler.

Compared to Beanstalk, App Runner reduces web/container infrastructure management; Beanstalk allows accessing and customizing the underlying EC2/ASG/Load Balancer resources.

Updated from April 30, 2026: App Runner is not accepting new customers; Existing customers continue to use it. AWS recommends ECS Express Mode for new needs; still keep the App Runner concept to learn SAA-C03 material.

8. Use cases

Use cases:

  • Team wants to deploy web app quickly but still uses EC2/ASG.
  • The Web tier writes the job to SQS and the Worker tier processes it.
  • Traditional applications need a managed platform rather than building the infrastructure yourself.

9. Compare App Runner vs Elastic Beanstalk vs ECS Express Mode & Deployment Type

ServiceInputInfrastructure management level
AWS App RunnerSource code or Container imageFully managed (HTTPS, scaling, automatic LB); Minimum configuration required
AWS Elastic BeanstalkApplication code / packageAWS builds EC2 + ALB + ASG but you still retain SSH/customization of the underlying EC2
ECS Express ModeContainer image + IAM RolesQuickly create ECS Service yourself on Fargate + ALB + Auto Scaling + HTTPS in your account
Rolling Deployment → Gradually replace instances/tasks on the existing environment
Blue/Green Deployment → Create a new environment in parallel and then transfer traffic (instant rollback)
Quick review · SAA-C03

Beanstalk builds its own web/worker infrastructure but still runs on AWS resources; Web Tier receives HTTP, Worker Tier handles async via SQS.

RDS, Aurora & ElastiCache

Amazon RDS fundamentals & operations

Purpose: Run a relational database without having to manage the entire server, patches and backups yourself.

Database flow:

Application
↓ Database endpoint
RDS
↓
Managed database / storage / backup

1. What does RDS manage, what do you manage?

RDS supports engines such as MySQL, PostgreSQL, MariaDB, Oracle, SQL Server and Db2; Aurora is a MySQL/PostgreSQL compatible option with its own architecture.

AWS takes care of provisioning, OS patching, and configured managed features.

You still design the schema, query, index, database users, capacity, backup retention and network security.

2. Configure database

Select engine/version, instance class, storage, subnet group, SG, parameter/option group and maintenance window.

Set DB in private subnets; Just put the SG app into the database port.

Changing instance class increases CPU/RAM; Add replica extension read.

Storage Auto Scaling increases disk within the set maximum storage, does not automatically increase CPU and does not automatically reduce disk.

3. Storage Auto Scaling

RDS increases storage when low free space is persistent and enough time has elapsed since the previous change.

Typical conditions: less than 10% left, lasts at least 5 minutes, has been at least 6 hours since the change or storage optimization has been completed, set a later date.

Set maximum large enough and alarm monitors storage. Auto Scaling does not replace data cleaning or capacity planning.

4. RDS vs. EC2 and RDS Custom

RDS usually does not allow SSH into the OS. DB on EC2 gives more control but you take care of patching, backup and HA yourself.

RDS Custom for Oracle/SQL Server needs deeper customization in OS/database. Changes must still be within the scope of support and coordination with the service's automation.

5. CloudWatch and Enhanced Monitoring

RDS monitoring modeCollection Cycle & Mechanism
RDS CloudWatch Metrics (Default)Send metrics at each hypervisor/database layer 1 minute (no need to enable EC2 Detailed Monitoring)
Enhanced MonitoringThe agent right on the DB instance collects deep OS/process metrics with cycles down to 1 second and send it back CloudWatch Logs (need monitoring IAM role)

Console refreshes fastest every 5 seconds even though collection interval is 1 second; Get 1-second updates via CloudWatch Logs. ·

6. Use cases

Use cases: Web needs managed PostgreSQL/MySQL: RDS. Enterprise software needs OS customization: consider RDS Custom. Need HA: add Multi-AZ; need to reduce reading load: Read Replica; need to reduce repeated queries: cache.

7. RDS Storage Types, Parameter Groups, Private Endpoint & RDS Custom

RDS StoragePerformance characteristics
gp2Previous General Purpose SSD; IOPS is tied to capacity + burst credits
gp3General Purpose SSD; Separately configure IOPS and throughput independently of capacity
io1 / io2 Block ExpressProvisioned IOPS SSD reserves stable, low-latency IOPS for heavy DB workloads
RDS componentsFunctions & Distinguishing Points
RDS Parameter GroupsWhere to configure engine parameters and DB security (eg limit connections, force SSL/TLS connections like rds.force_ssl)
RDS Private EndpointDNS hostname of DB resolves to Private IP in subnet when Public Access is turned off (not Interface VPC Endpoint)
RDS Custom for Oracle / SQL ServerManaged service but allows permissions privileged OS & DB access to install third-party software/plugins or use special BYOL
Quick review · SAA-C03

RDS is a managed relational database: AWS takes care of much of the operational work, while schema/query design and application optimization are still your responsibility.
