RDS Multi-AZ & Read Replicas

Purpose: Distinguish fault-tolerant solutions from solutions that increase read capacity.

Distinguish between two needs:

Multi-AZ = fault tolerance/failover
Read Replica = increased read load

1. Key comparison

CriteriaMulti-AZ DB instanceRead Replica
TargetAvailability and automatic failoverRead scaling, reporting, can support DR
ReplicationSynchronous to standby in another AZAsynchronous, has replica lag
Standby serving reading?NoThere is an endpoint for the app to send read queries
When primary failsRDS moves the endpoint to standbyPromote according to the appropriate process, change the app endpoint

2. Multi-AZ DB instance

The application uses a stable DB endpoint. When primary fails, AWS failsover to standby; app needs to retry/reconnect and handle interrupted transactions.

Multi-AZ in a Region does not protect against the failure of the entire Region and does not replace backups to prevent accidental deletion of data.

3. Don't mistake Multi-AZ DB cluster

RDS Multi-AZ DB cluster: Another model: one writer and two readable standbys across three AZs, using semisynchronous replication.

It supports both HA and read load; The statement “all Multi-AZ standbys are unreadable” does not apply. AWS differentiates between two models.

4. Read Replica

Replica can be in the same or different Region when the engine supports it.

The app must send the read query to the replica endpoint; query requires the latest data so use primary when necessary.

Promote replica creates an independent DB, no longer following the old primary.
The number of replicas and features depends on the engine.
Replication in the same Region is free to transfer according to the RDS support mechanism;
cross-Region has a transfer fee.

5. Use cases

Use cases: DB production needs to failover itself when AZ fails: Multi-AZ. Heavy reports slow down transactions: Read Replica. Users in other Regions need to read more closely or have DR: cross-Region replica with promotion plan.

6. Reconciliation table of RTO / RPO / Failover (RDS & Aurora) & Aurora Read Replica from RDS

Deployment modelCommon RTORPOFailover Mechanism
RDS Read ReplicaSeveral minutes or moreDepends on replication lagManual promotion
RDS Multi-AZ DB Instance60–120 seconds0 (synchronous)Automatic
RDS Multi-AZ DB ClusterUnder 35 seconds0 (semi-sync quorum)Automatic
Aurora DB Cluster + ReplicaUsually less than 30 seconds0 (shared storage 6 copies/3 AZ)Automatic
Aurora Global Database (Cross-Region)Under 1 minute (managed)Usually < 1 second (async)The Managed/Manual Failover step needs to be enabled to promote the secondary cluster

Migration with less downtime: can create Aurora Read Replica directly from the running RDS MySQL/PostgreSQL, wait for replication to catch up and then promote to the independent Aurora cluster.

Quick review · SAA-C03

Multi-AZ prioritizes high availability/failover; Read Replica prioritizes scale reads. Read Replica does not replace the backup or HA synchronization mechanism.

Amazon Aurora architecture & scaling

Purpose: Use a MySQL/PostgreSQL compatible database with distributed storage and integrated read scaling.

Two access routes:

Write → Writer endpoint → Primary instance
Read → Reader endpoint → Aurora Replicas
Compute → shared distributed storage

1. Compute is separate from storage

Aurora clusters typically have one writer and up to 15 Aurora Replicas. Instances share distributed storage; Storage expands automatically as data increases.

Storage holds 6 replicas across 3 AZs and automatically corrects failed replicas.

The quorum mechanism is often described as 4 copies for writing, 3 copies for reading; Storage copies are not 6 database instances.

2. Writer and Reader endpoints

EndpointUse when
Writer/cluster endpointWrite or read-write; Points to the current writer
Reader endpointDistribute read connections across replicas
Instance endpointNeed a specific instance
Custom endpointSplit replica groups for a workload

The Reader endpoint is balanced at the connection level, not dividing each SQL query itself.

Without a replica, the reader endpoint can point to the writer; Do not consider the endpoint name as a read-only enforcement mechanism.

3. Failover

When the writer fails, Aurora can promote the replica and update the writer endpoint.

Placing the replica in another AZ helps restore compute faster; Multi-AZ storage by itself does not guarantee uninterrupted connectivity.

App still needs reconnect/retry. Actual failover time depends on the situation; don't consider the quick numbers in the slide as an SLA for every defect.

4. Auto Scaling and custom endpoints

Aurora Auto Scaling increases/decreases the number of readers according to metrics such as CPU or connections. It increases read capacity, unlike Serverless v2 which adjusts the compute capacity of the instance.

Custom endpoints can push heavy reporting into a more powerful replica pool. Reader endpoint still exists; app selects endpoints according to workload.

5. Use cases

Use cases: OLTP requires MySQL/PostgreSQL-compatible and HA; Read-intensive SaaS uses reader endpoint; Separate reporting from transaction query using replica/custom endpoint.

Quick review · SAA-C03

Aurora has its own storage and replication architecture; Reader Endpoint serves read queries, Writer Endpoint is for writes.

Aurora Serverless, Global & advanced capabilities

Purpose: Choose Aurora features that are appropriate for fluctuating loads, global users, and dev/test environments.

1. Aurora Serverless v2

Serverless v2 automatically adjusts compute capacity according to load within the min/max ACU range
configured.
Unpredictable load matching; Still need to choose engine, network, security and limits
capacity. capacity.

Unlike the Auto Scaling: Serverless v2 reader that changes the capacity of the instance, the Auto Scaling reader changes the number of replicas.

The ability to scale to 0/auto-pause depends on engine version and configuration, not by default every cluster stops when idle.

2. Aurora Global Database

Primary Region receives records; Secondary Regions receive data asynchronously to read near users and serve DR.

When the main Region fails, failover/promote the backup Region according to the Global Database process.

Primary Region: writer → replication → Secondary Region: local reads
                                      └─ promote/failover khi DR
Replication is usually very fast but asynchronous still carries the risk of losing changes
not replicated yet.
Evaluate RPO/RTO and failover testing; Do not consider Multi-AZ a substitute for it
cross-Region DR.

3. Database Cloning

Clone uses copy-on-write: initially shares the same data blocks, only providing new blocks when there are changes. Creating a test environment is faster than copying the entire database.

Clone has its own compute and still has costs; query test runs on clone instead
writer production.
Do not consider clones as independent backups instead of snapshots/DRs.

4. Babelfish and Machine Learning

Babelfish helps Aurora PostgreSQL understand part of T-SQL/TDS to reduce the effort to repair SQL Server applications; Must check compatibility features, not guaranteed all apps run as they are.

Aurora ML can call SageMaker/Comprehend via SQL for use case support. This is an integration, not every database has its own prediction model.

5. Use cases

Use cases: Erratic load: Serverless v2. Local global reads and DR: Global Database. Production version for dev/test: clone. Migration of SQL Server needs to reduce code changes: Babelfish review.

6. Aurora Standard vs Aurora I/O-Optimized

Configure Aurora StorageHow to calculate feesWhen is it optimal?
Aurora StandardCharge for GB-month capacity + charge per read/write I/O operationWorkload has a moderate amount of I/O
Aurora I/O-OptimizedCompute & storage prices are higher but Completely free for each I/O requestI/O overhead exceeds approx 25% of total Aurora bill or need to forecast stable costs for large I/O loads
Quick review · SAA-C03

Aurora Serverless addresses the need to scale compute; Aurora Global Database optimizes multi-Region reading and DR strategies, which does not mean multi-Region automatic writing.

RDS/Aurora backup, restore, security & RDS Proxy

Purpose: Restore data, protect connections and avoid database being overloaded by many connections.

1. Automated backups and snapshots

Automated backup combines backups with transaction logs for Point-in-Time Restore (PITR) in the retention window.

RDS DB instances support retention for up to 35 days; Set 0 to disable automated backup in enabled models.

Aurora keeps continuous backups according to configured retention.

Manual snapshots are kept until they are automatically deleted.

Restore creates a new DB/cluster, does not overwrite the running DB; must move the application to the new endpoint after checking the data.

2. Restore and migrate

Wrongly deleting data → PITR before the problem → new DB → check → change app endpoint

Some MySQL restores from S3 use backups created by Percona XtraBackup, requiring correct version/format support. You don't have to upload any dump files to S3 to restore.

Snapshot is different from read replica: replica can replicate mistakenly deleted operations, while backup allows returning to a previous point in time.

3. Network, credentials and encryption

Set DB private, SG only receives from app.
TLS encryption in transit; KMS encrypts when saving and backing up.
If you want to encrypt the old database, usually snapshot → encrypted copy → new restore.

Secrets Manager saves/rotates passwords; IAM DB authentication uses tokens for DB users configured on the supporting engine.

RDS API management permissions do not automatically grant permissions to run SQL in the DB.

4. RDS Proxy

Proxy pool/reuse connection, useful when Lambda/application opens many short connections.

The proxy resides in the VPC, helping to reduce connection storms and improve failover handling; Does not cache SQL results or make heavy queries automatically faster.

Lambda/EC2 → RDS Proxy → connection pool → RDS/Aurora

Passwords can be used with Secrets Manager or IAM authentication according to supported configuration.

IAM is not always required for clients; End-to-end IAM can also unstore passwords in Secrets Manager. AWS RDS Proxy authentication.

5. Operational events and data events

RDS Event Notifications/EventBridge reports DB events such as failover, snapshot creation or state change; Not a stream that changes each row.

Some engines support calling Lambda from SQL/DB logic.
Requires both IAM permission and network connection to Lambda; Private DB can use NAT/VPC
Appropriate endpoints.
If you need CDC, use the CDC tool/stream, don't rely on lifecycle notifications.

6. Use cases

Use cases: Accidentally deleted recovery: PITR. Lambda opened too many connections: RDS Proxy. DB security: private subnet + SG + TLS + KMS + secret management. Failover warning: RDS event → SNS/EventBridge.

7. IAM Database Authentication & Cross-VPC RDS Proxy

Authentication/Connection mechanismHow it Works & Conditions
IAM Database AuthenticationAuthenticate into RDS/Aurora (MySQL, PostgreSQL, MariaDB) using Temporary auth token is valid for 15 minutes instead of static password; Request to enable features on the DB, create the corresponding DB user (eg AWSAuthenticationPlugin on MySQL), IAM permissions rds-db:connect and connect via TLS (open connection is not terminated when token expires 15 minutes)
Cross-VPC RDS Proxy EndpointAllow Lambda/EC2 in VPC A to connect to RDS Proxy and Private RDS in VPC B via ENI + Private IP (AWS PrivateLink) without needing to open a public DB or combine CIDR across the network
EC2 / Lambda (IAM Role with rds-db:connect)
  ↓ Generate IAM Auth Token (15 minutes)
  ↓ TLS connection
RDS / Aurora (reconcile DB User + IAM Token)
Quick review · SAA-C03

Backup/PITR is used to restore data; Multi-AZ to increase BP; RDS Proxy to manage and reuse application connections to the database.

Amazon ElastiCache

Purpose: Keep frequently used data in RAM for quick response and reduce the number of database queries.

1. Cache-aside

App → cache: has data → returns results
          └─ not available → read DB → write cache with TTL → return result
The application must add cache read/write logic; ElastiCache does not front itself
database like RDS Proxy.
Select TTL and invalidation to avoid returning old data for too long.

Write-through updates the cache at the same time as writing to the DB, making data available for reading but increasing write work and possibly caching unused data.

Cache-aside only loads when needed but the first read has a cache miss.

2. Redis/Valkey and Memcached

ChooseMain point
Redis OSS/ValkeyData structures, sorted sets, replication/read replicas, Multi-AZ failover and snapshots depending on configuration
Node-based MemcachedSimple key-value cache, sharding, multi-thread; no native replication/failover like Redis

Node-based Memcached does not have backup like Redis/Valkey; Serverless Memcached has snapshot/restore support.

So we need to look at both the engine and the deployment model. AWS backup support.

3. Sessions and leaderboards

Storing sessions in cache helps multiple app instances share the same login state. Redis/Valkey sorted sets are suitable for leaderboards because they store scores and query rankings quickly.

Common read cache reduces repeated queries on RDS.

Read Replica still runs SQL on the DB data; cache stores results/objects for quick retrieval, two solutions can be used together.

4. Security and data may be lost

Use private network, source-limited SG, TLS and appropriate authentication engine/version: AUTH/RBAC or IAM authentication when supported.

IAM cluster management and IAM data authentication are different permissions/configurations.

Designed to tolerate cache misses or cache data loss; For data that needs to be sustainable, there must be a data source/backup that meets the requirements.

Asynchronous replication does not guarantee that all writes will not be lost in case of error.

5. Use cases

Use cases: Product catalog read a lot: cache-aside. Multiple EC2 web needs common session: Redis/Valkey. Game ranking: sorted sets. Simple, renewable cache: Memcached.

Quick review · SAA-C03

ElastiCache Redis/Valkey or Memcached places data in RAM to reduce backend load and reduce latency; DAX is specific to DynamoDB.

Purpose-Built Databases

Amazon DocumentDB

Purpose: Amazon DocumentDB is a managed document database compatible with MongoDB API, used to store data in JSON document format with flexible schema.

DocumentDB = Document Database = MongoDB-compatible = Managed by AWS

1. Document Model

Other relational databases: RDS / Aurora = Table = Row / Column = Clear Schema

DocumentDB: Document = JSON-like object = each document can have different fields

For example:

Product A
{
  name: "Phone",
  color: "Black"
}

Product B
{
  name: "Laptop",
  ram: "16GB",
  cpu: "M3"
}

→ No need for all records to have the same column.

2. MongoDB Compatibility

Applications can use:

MongoDB Driver
↓
DocumentDB

But remember: DocumentDB ≠ MongoDB self-hosted

DocumentDB = AWS service compatible with MongoDB API

So still need to check which MongoDB features are supported.

3. Managed Cluster

AWS admin: Provisioning · Patching · Backup · Replication · Storage scaling

Application still has to worry about: Document Design · Indexes · Queries · Security

4. Availability & Storage

DocumentDB has replication across multiple AZs:

Application
↓
DocumentDB Cluster
↓
Replicas across AZs

Objective: High Availability + Failover

Storage can expand automatically as data increases.

5. Index

If you want to query quickly, you still need an index.

Query by email
↓
Index: email
↓
Search faster

If there is no suitable index:

Full Scan
↓
Query is slow

Managed database does not mean automatically optimized queries.

6. DocumentDB vs RDS vs Neptune

  • RDS / Aurora = SQL = relational = join multiple tables
  • DocumentDB = JSON document = flexible schema = MongoDB-compatible
  • Neptune = Graph Database = relationship / traversal
Bank Transaction
→ RDS / Aurora

Product Catalog
→ DocumentDB

Social Network Relationship
→ Neptune

7. Security

DocumentDB typically runs in:

VPC
↓
Security Group
↓
TLS
↓
DocumentDB Cluster

IAM is used to manage AWS resources, but permissions to read/write database data still require appropriate authentication/authorization mechanisms.

8. Use Cases

Use cases:

Product Catalog
↓
DocumentDB
User Profile
↓
DocumentDB
CMS / Content Metadata
↓
DocumentDB
MongoDB-compatible Application
↓
DocumentDB

9. Document Size & DocumentDB Serverless

DocumentDB supports maximum document size 16 MB and there is a regime DocumentDB Serverless Self-scaling capacity every second based on DCU.

Quick review · SAA-C03

DocumentDB = document database compatible with MongoDB API; RDS/Aurora = relational SQL; Neptune = graph. Managed does not mean the query is self-optimized.

Amazon Neptune

Purpose: Amazon Neptune is a managed graph database used when data has many relationships and connections between them need to be queried.

Neptune = Graph Database = Node + Relationship

1. Graph Model

Graph includes: Node / Vertex = entity

Edge = relationship between entities

For example:

User A
↓ FRIEND_OF
User B
↓ BOUGHT
Product X

Or:

User
↓ LIKES
Post
↓ HAS_COMMENT
Comment

Neptune is strong when the question needs to go through many relationships.

2. When to use Neptune?

Use cases:

User A
↓ friend
User B
↓ bought
Product X

Query: "What product did User A's friend buy?"

Neptune can traverse the graph directly instead of having to join multiple tables.

3. Use Cases

Social Network
↓
Friend / Like / Share relationships
Fraud Detection
↓
Account → Device → Transaction → Address
↓
Look for suspicious connections
Recommendation
↓
User → Product → Related Product
Knowledge Graph
↓
Entity → Relationship → Entity

4. Availability

AWS admin: Cluster · Replication · Backup · Failover

Read replicas can be used to scale reading workload.

5. Neptune Streams

Neptune Streams = records changes in the graph sequentially.

Graph changes
↓
Neptune Streams
↓
Consumer/Lambda
↓
OpenSearch / S3 / Cache
User A LIKE Product X
↓
Neptune Streams
↓
Lambda
↓
Update Recommendation Index

No need to poll the entire graph to find out what just changed.

6. Neptune vs Other Databases

  • RDS / Aurora = relational SQL
  • DocumentDB = JSON document
  • Neptune = relationship / graph traversal
  • Timestream = data over time

7. Graph query language: SPARQL vs Gremlin/openCypher

Data model on NeptuneQuery language
RDF (Resource Description Framework) — triples & knowledge graphSPARQL
Property Graph — Vertices and edges carry attributesApache TinkerPop Gremlin or openCypher
Quick review · SAA-C03

Neptune is for data with many relationships that need traversal; Different from relational SQL database and document database.

Amazon Timestream

Purpose: Amazon Timestream is a serverless time-series database used to store and analyze timestamp data.

Timestream = Time Series Database = Timestamp + Measurements

1. Time Series Data

For example:

10:00 → CPU = 40%
10:01 → CPU = 55%
10:02 → CPU = 80%

Or IoT: Timestamp · DeviceId · Temperature · Humidity · Battery

Time is the most important part of data.

2. Data Model

A record usually has: Timestamp + Dimensions + Measures

Timestamp = 10:00 · Dimensions: · deviceId = sensor-01 · region = ap-southeast-1 · Measures: · temperature = 30 · humidity = 70

3. Multi-measure Record

If multiple measurements have the same timestamp:

10:00 · Temperature = 30 · Humidity = 70 · Battery = 90%

Can be saved together: 1 Timestamp + many Measures

→ reduce the number of records/writes.

4. Storage Tiering

Timestream divides the data itself:

Recent Data
↓
Memory Store
= query nhanh
Old Data
↓
Historical Storage
= cheaper

You can still query both.

5. Query

Timestream supports SQL for temporal analysis.

Average CPU per 5 minutes · Highest temperature today · Request latency in the last 1 hour

Time-series Data
↓
Timestream
↓
SQL / Aggregation
↓
Dashboard / Analytics

6. Scheduled Query

The query can be run periodically:

Raw CPU Metrics
↓
Scheduled Query
↓
Average CPU every 5 minutes
↓
Summary Table

Suitable for creating aggregates for dashboards.

7. Popular flow

↓

↓

Route 53 & Hybrid DNS

DNS & Amazon Route 53 fundamentals

Purpose: Use easy-to-remember domain names instead of IPs and manage how DNS returns application endpoints.

1. How does DNS work?

DNS translates a name like api.example.com to an IP or destination name.

Resolver asks name servers when there is no cache; The authoritative name server is where the official answer for the zone is located.

Client → DNS resolver → Root → TLD (.com) → authoritative server
       ← IP is cached according to TTL
Client → connects directly to the application

Route 53: managed authoritative DNS, supporting domain registration and health-aware routing.

It returns a DNS answer, not a proxy web request.

2. Records need to be distinguished

RecordMeaning
AName → IPv4
AAAAName → IPv6
CNAMEName → other name; Do not use at zone apex
NSName server is responsible for zone/delegation
Alias of Route 53Point to a supporting AWS resource or appropriate record in the zone; Can be used at apex

For example example.com → ALB uses Alias; www.example.com can use CNAME.

Alias does not arbitrarily point to every hostname outside of AWS like CNAME and is not a direct way to point to EC2's IP using Alias.

3. TTL

TTL: the time the resolver/client can cache the answer.

High TTL reduces DNS queries but is slow to reflect changes; Low TTL supports faster endpoint changes but increases queries.

Record includes name, type, value/target, TTL when applicable and routing policy. Changing DNS does not close open connections and does not force all caches to update immediately.

4. Public and Private Hosted Zones

Public zones serve public domain names on the Internet.

Private zone serving VPC is associated and hybrid network through appropriate Resolver; private names do not automatically open network access to the application.

To get private DNS working in VPC, enable the necessary DNS properties and check the association. A hosted zone only contains records, not where the website is stored.

5. Registrar is different from DNS service

You can buy a domain from another registrar and then use Route 53: create a public hosted zone, update the name servers in the registrar according to the zone's NS.

It is not required to transfer domain ownership to AWS.

6. Use cases

Use cases: Root domain pointing to ALB: Alias. Internal name for database/service: Private Hosted Zone. Public domain purchased outside of AWS: delegate DNS to Route 53.

7. Route 53 A Alias Record Support Destination

Route 53 Alias Record Can be used right away zone apex (example.com — where not created CNAME) and freely point the query to two target groups: (1) AWS Resources is supported (ALB/NLB, CloudFront, API Gateway, S3 Website Endpoint, Global Accelerator, Elastic Beanstalk, VPC Interface Endpoint) and (2) another DNS Record of the same type in the same Hosted Zone.

Quick review · SAA-C03

DNS translates domain names into records; Route 53 manages hosted zones, DNS records, and routing policy. Public and private hosted zones address different needs.

Route 53 routing policies

Purpose: Select endpoints returned in DNS by rollout rate, latency, location or health.

DNS Flow:

Client
↓ DNS query
Route 53 routing policy
↓ returns the appropriate endpoint
Client connects to endpoint

1. Simple

A record has one or more values; The client chooses when receiving multiple values.

Simple does not attach health checks directly to records; Alias ​​has Evaluate Target Health according to supported target types.

Suitable for a simple endpoint, no need for complex selection policies.

2. Weighted

Assign weight to records with the same name/type.
Weight 1 and 9 give a DNS answer rate of about 10%/90%; There is no need for the total weight to be equal
100.
DNS cache makes the actual request rate not absolutely accurate.

Weight 0 is usually not selected when there is a healthy positive weight record.

If all positive record weights are unhealthy, record weight 0 can be considered; If all weights are 0, divide equally.

Do not use weight 0 as an access ban mechanism.

3. Latency-based

Choose the Region with the best measured latency for the query side, not necessarily the closest by geographical distance.

Suitable for apps running multiple regions that need quick response, can combine health to eliminate error endpoints.

4. Failover

Active-passive: if primary is healthy, return primary; If primary fails, switch to secondary according to configured health. Suitable for DR site or backup notification site.

5. Geolocation

Select by queryer's location: continent, country or US state. More specific regions are preferred; create default record for unmatched location.

Content/language matching by country.

DNS routing is not a mechanism to prevent unauthorized access; want to enforce permissions/areas that need to be controlled in the application/edge.

6. Geoproximity

Choose according to the distance between user and resource, with bias to expand/retract service area. Positive bias increases resource traffic, negative bias decreases. Other geolocation is divided by national borders and latency is selected by latency.

7. IP-based

Use CIDR collection to map the query source/client address provided by DNS to the location/endpoint.

Suitable when you know the company's/ISP's IP range and want your own route; The DNS resolver and EDNS client subnet can affect the addresses Route 53 sees.

8. Multi-Value Answer

Return up to 8 healthy records per query when configuring health check. The client connects itself to a value; There is no connection management proxy like ELB.

9. Use cases

Use cases: Canary about 10%: Weighted. Global app prioritizes latency: Latency-based. By language/country: Geolocation. Change service area by bias: Geoproximity. Primary/backup: Failover.

Quick review · SAA-C03

Simple/Weighted/Latency/Failover/Geolocation/Geoproximity/Multivalue are chosen according to traffic navigation goals, not the same type of load balancing.

Route 53 health checks & failover

Purpose: Use health checks to select healthy endpoints and switch to a backup location when a failure occurs.

1. Three ways to determine health

WayWhen used
Endpoint health checkPublic HTTP/HTTPS/TCP endpoint
Calculated health checkCombine the results of multiple health check children
CloudWatch alarm health checkMetric/alarm signal, useful for private resources
Route 53 health checker on the Internet does not go directly to the internal private IP
VPC.
With private apps, you can use monitoring inside VPC → metric/alarm → health
check.

2. Evaluate Target Health

Alias to supported resources such as ELBs can obtain the target's health using Evaluate Target Health, without creating a separate Route 53 endpoint check.

Not all alias targets support this; CloudFront does not support Evaluate Target Health = Yes.

Check the health of a specific endpoint, avoid checking the domain itself that is failing over because the results will depend on its DNS routing. AWS health-check configuration.

3. Active-passive failover

Primary healthy → DNS returns primary
Primary unhealthy → DNS returns secondary according to configured health

Primary needs appropriate health signal: health check or Evaluate Target Health with Alias support. Secondary must be truly ready to serve and have data/capacity that meets DR.

4. DNS has cache

Failover DNS only changes the answer for the next query; Do not transfer current connection
open.
TTL, error detection time, and client retry all affect interruption time
paragraph.

If all endpoints are unhealthy, Route 53 has fail-open behavior depending on the policy; Do not consider health check as a firewall that always blocks all faulty endpoints.

5. Use cases

Use cases: Active-passive between two Regions; primary ALB and static recovery page; Calculated checks reflect multiple dependencies. Try actual DR, checking both DNS and database and application status.

Quick review · SAA-C03

Route 53 health check supports DNS failover; DNS caching/TTL affects redirection speed and DNS does not automatically regenerate failed infrastructure.

Hybrid DNS & Route 53 Resolver

Purpose: For AWS and on-premises to resolve each other's private domain names.

1. Resolver in VPC

Route 53 Resolver handles AWS-provided names, public DNS and associated Private Hosted Zone. VPN/Direct Connect connection creates a network connection but does not automatically make all private DNS on both sides understand each other.

2. Inbound endpoint

Inbound: query direction going into AWS: DNS on-prem forward domain AWS/private zone to the IP of the inbound endpoint.

On-prem machine → DNS on-prem → VPN/DX → Inbound endpoint → Private Hosted Zone

There is no need to turn private records into public for internal users to look up.

3. Outbound endpoint

Outbound: Query direction going out of AWS: Resolver forwarding rule selects suffix like corp.example.com and then sends query through outbound endpoint to DNS on-prem.

EC2 → VPC Resolver → rule for corp.example.com → Outbound → DNS on-prem

The rule must be associated with the VPC in use; can share cross-account rules via AWS RAM.

4. Availability and network

Use IP endpoints in at least two AZs, SGs for DNS TCP/UDP 53 and VPN/DX lines to the DNS server. Check route/firewall both ways.

Do not forward the same domain around to both sides and then back to yourself. Resolvable DNS still needs route/SG to connect to the return IP.

5. Use cases

Use cases: On-prem look up the private database name in AWS: Inbound. EC2 looks up Active Directory domain on-prem: Outbound + forwarding rule. Multiple accounts share the same hybrid DNS: share rules.

6. Three types of Resolver Rule: Forward Rule, System Rule & Delegation Rule

Ingredients / RulesDistinguishing function
Inbound EndpointReceive DNS queries from On-premises going into VPC Resolver (Private Hosted Zone resolution)
Outbound Endpoint + Forward RulePass the DNS query of the specified domain (eg corp.example.com) from VPC to DNS Server On-premises IP
System RuleKeep a specific subdomain (eg aws.corp.example.com) back to AWS VPC Resolver to process, causing an exception to override the parent Forward Rule
Delegation RuleAuthorize subdomain resolution based on records NS to the authoritative DNS server via Outbound Endpoint
Quick review · SAA-C03

Inbound Resolver Endpoint: query from on-prem to AWS; Outbound Endpoint + forwarding rule: query from VPC to DNS on-prem.

Amazon S3

Amazon S3 fundamentals, buckets & websites

Purpose: Amazon S3 is an object storage service for storing files, backups, logs, data lakes and static content with very high durability and scalability.

S3 = Object Storage = File / Backup / Log / Data Lake

1. Bucket & Object

S3 saves data according to:

Bucket
↓
Object

Objects include: Key · Value (data/file) · Metadata

For example:

Bucket: company-data · images/logo.png · logs/2026/app.log · reports/report.pdf

images/ good logs/ just prefix logic, not the real filesystem folder.

Bucket = container, Object = file/data, Key = name/path of object.

2. Bucket & Region

Bucket is created in an AWS Region.

S3 Bucket
↓
ap-southeast-1

Bucket names according to the S3 namespace must normally be unique.

Some important rules: 3–63 characters · lowercase · no underscore _ · not the same as IP address

S3 looks like a global service, but bucket's data is in the selected Region.

3. Object Size

One maximum object: 5 TB

Large files should use: Multipart Upload

Large File
↓
divided into many parts
↓
parallel upload
↓
S3 reassembled

4. Consistency

S3 provided strong consistency for: PUT · DELETE · LIST

Upload objects
↓
GET/LIST immediately after
↓
see new version

5. S3 is not a File System

S3 is not: EBS = block storage · EFS = file system

The application accesses S3 via: API · SDK · HTTP · AWS CLI

6. Use Cases

Use cases:

Backup / Archive · Logs · Data Lake · Images / Videos · Static Assets · Software Distribution · Disaster Recovery

Commonly used long-term archives: S3 Glacier storage classes

7. Static Website Hosting

S3 can host: HTML · CSS · JavaScript · Images

But S3 website endpoint mainly uses:

HTTP

Production is usually better:

User
↓ HTTPS
CloudFront
↓
Private S3 Bucket

with: OAC = Origin Access Control

→ S3 does not need to be published directly.

8. S3 Security

There are two common classes of permissions: IAM Policy = What can principal do?

Bucket Policy = who can access bucket/object

IAM User
↓
IAM Allow s3:GetObject
↓
S3

or cross-account:

Account A
↓
Bucket Policy
↓
Bucket Account B

Principle: Explicit Deny · > all Allows

9. Block Public Access

This is a guardrail against accidentally publicizing a bucket/object.

Block Public Access = block public access

If you want to static public website directly using S3, you must configure public access accordingly.

Production often prioritizes: Private S3 + CloudFront

10. Object Ownership & ACL

Modern S3 often uses: Bucket Owner Enforced

→ ACL is disabled.

Main rights are governed by: IAM Policy · Bucket Policy · Access Point Policy

Prioritize policies instead of ACLs.

11. Pre-Signed URL

Used to give users access temporary private object.

Download:

Backend
↓
Generate Pre-Signed URL
↓
User
↓
GET private object

Upload:

Backend
↓
Pre-Signed PUT URL
↓
Browser
↓
Upload directly to S3

Object still: Private ✅

The URL is only valid for the specified time.

Pre-signed URL ≠ public bucket.

12. S3 Access Points

Access Point creates separate endpoint + policy for each application/team.

An S3 Bucket
├── /finance/*
├── /sales/*
└── /analytics/*

Can create:

Finance Access Point
→ /finance/*

Sales Access Point
→ /sales/*

Analytics Access Point
→ read necessary data

→ avoid a policy bucket that is too large.

13. VPC Access Point

You can limit access only from VPC:

EC2 in VPC
↓
S3 VPC Endpoint
↓
VPC-only Access Point
↓
S3

Suitable when you want: S3 access · does not go to the Internet

14. S3 Object Lambda

Allowed transform object at GET without modifying the original object.

Application
↓
Object Lambda Access Point
↓
Lambda
↓
Transform
↓
Response
Redact PII
Resize image
Add watermark
XML → JSON

Original Object: no change

Quick review · SAA-C03

S3 stores objects in buckets, not traditional block/file systems; public website, bucket policy and presigned URL are different mechanisms.

S3 encryption, HTTPS & CORS

Purpose: Protect content stored in S3, encrypt transmission and let the browser access the correct origin.

1. Encryption at rest

WayWho manages the key?Point to understand
SSE-S3S3/AWSAES-256 server-side encryption; default for new objects
SSE-KMSAWS KMSControl key policy, audit key usage, have KMS permission/quota/cost
SSE-CCustomersSend key per request over HTTPS; S3 does not save keys for future use
Client-sideApplication/clientEncrypt before upload; S3 only receives ciphertext

Encryption does not grant access on its own and does not change bucket policy. Changing the default encryption does not automatically re-encrypt any old objects with the new type/key.

2. SSE-KMS

Upload needs appropriate permissions like kms:GenerateDataKey; reading requires kms:Decrypt.
Cross-account access requires S3 permission and customer managed KMS key
permission from the other side.

KMS's request rate may become limited when S3 traffic is large.

S3 Bucket Keys help reduce KMS requests and SSE-KMS costs when appropriate; must still keep the correct key policy.

3. HTTPS

TLS/HTTPS protects transmission. Bucket policy can be explicit Deny if aws:SecureTransport=false to deny HTTP.

Client --HTTPS--> S3 --server-side encryption--> object is saved
S3 website endpoint does not support HTTPS directly; Use CloudFront for your website
HTTPS.
SSE-C requires HTTPS and must keep the key secure. If the key is lost, it cannot be decrypted.

4. CORS

Origin includes protocol, host and port. The web at https://app.example.com calling S3 at another origin needs CORS on the destination bucket with the appropriate origin/method/header.

CORS is a browser rule for reading responses, does not grant S3 permissions and does not prevent any clients outside the browser. Pre-signed URL/bucket policy must still allow action first.

5. Use cases

Use cases: Need control/audit key: SSE-KMS. Browser uploads directly to S3: pre-signed PUT + CORS. Data must be encrypted before reaching S3: client-side. Only for secure transmission: policy enforces HTTPS.

Quick review · SAA-C03

SSE-S3/SSE-KMS/SSE-C is server-side encryption; HTTPS protects in transit; CORS is a browser mechanism, not IAM permission.

S3 versioning, replication & immutable protection

Purpose: Recover an overwritten/wrongly deleted object that has a copy elsewhere and keep the data from being destroyed prematurely.

Three different mechanisms:

Versioning = keeping versions
Replication = copy to another bucket
Object Lock = version protection by retention

1. Versioning

Turn on in bucket to keep multiple versions of the same key. Overwrite creates new version; DELETE normally adds a delete marker, not immediately deleting old versions.

Restore by getting the old version or deleting the appropriate delete marker. Deleting with version ID can permanently delete that version. Suspend Versioning does not automatically delete existing versions.

2. CRR and SRR

CRR replicate to another Region; SRR replicates in the same Region.

Can cross-account, needs Versioning at source/destination with appropriate roles and policies; The SSE-KMS object also needs KMS configuration/permissions.

Asynchronous replication: does not promise data to appear immediately.
By default, the live replication rule applies new objects after enabling it; old object used
S3 Batch Replication.

3. Delete and replicate

Delete markers can be replicated if appropriate options and rules are enabled. Permanently deleting a version at the source does not automatically replicate that deletion to the destination.

There is no automatic chaining: object replicate A → B does not continue on its own to B → C
just because B has a rule to C.
If you want multiple destinations, configure appropriate replication from the source.

4. MFA Delete

MFA required when permanently deleting version or changing Versioning status. MFA is not required just to add a regular delete marker.

Bucket owner/root enables or disables MFA Delete via API/CLI with MFA, not via S3 Console. Versioning must be enabled.

5. Object Lock

WORM protects each version; Need Versioning.

Can be enabled for new or existing buckets according to the support process; Once enabled, do not turn off Object Lock/Versioning in the usual way.

MechanismMeaning
Governance retentionPeople with appropriate bypass permissions can bypass protection
Compliance retentionNo one, not even root, can remove/shorten the retention of the locked version
Legal holdHold until the person with the right to remove the hold; independent of retention date
Locking an existing version still allows writing a new version with the same key.
Legal hold does not expire; Expired retention may not be deleted if legal
hold is still on.

6. Use cases

Use cases: File is overwritten: Versioning. DR/compliance multiple Region: CRR. Anti-ransomware backups or records should remain intact: Object Lock, limited permissions and appropriate policy retention.

Quick review · SAA-C03

Versioning keeps multiple versions; Replication copies objects between buckets; Object Lock protects instances from deletion/editing according to retention.

S3 storage classes & lifecycle

Purpose: Choose the appropriate storage class to optimize costs based on:

Access frequency · Retrieval time · AZ number · Retention time · Ability to reproduce data

1. Overview of Storage Classes

  • S3 Standard = frequent visits
  • Standard-IA = less access = multi-AZ
  • One Zone-IA = low traffic = 1 AZ
  • Intelligent-Tiering = AWS automatically changes tiers according to access patterns
  • Glacier = archive / long-term storage
  • Express One Zone = 1 AZ = very low latency / high performance
  • Common points: Durability = 99.999999999% (11 nines)

Main differences: Availability · AZ · Storage cost · Retrieval cost · Retrieval time · Minimum storage duration

2. S3 Standard

S3 Standard = general purpose = frequent access = multi-AZ = low latency = high throughput

No: Retrieval fee · Minimum storage duration

Suitable: Active application data · Web content · Big Data · Mobile / Gaming

3. Standard-IA

IA = Infrequent Access

Used for data:

Less access
but when needed
→ must get it immediately

Standard-IA = multi-AZ = fast retrieval = cheaper storage Standard = has a retrieval fee

According to the slides: Minimum storage duration = 30 days

Use cases:

Backups · Disaster Recovery · Data is rarely accessed

4. One Zone-IA

One Zone-IA = same as IA but only stored in 1 AZ

Therefore:

Cheaper than Standard-IA
but
AZ is lost
→ data may be lost

Suitable when data: Can be recreated · or · already has another copy

For example:

Thumbnails · Secondary backup · Re-creatable data

Test score: need infrequent access + data can recreate → think about One Zone-IA.

5. S3 Intelligent-Tiering

Use when: Unknown · access pattern

AWS automatically tracks access and transfers objects between tiers.

Frequent Access
↓ no access for 30 days
Infrequent Access
↓ No longer access
Archive Instant Access

There are additional optional archive tiers: Archive Access · Deep Archive Access

Advantages: Automatically optimize storage cost · No need to manually write transitions according to access pattern · No retrieval fee between access tiers

Yes: Monitoring / automation fee

Test score: access pattern is unstable or unpredictable → Intelligent-Tiering.

6. S3 Glacier Classes

Used for: Archive · Backup · Long-term retention

6.1. Glacier Instant Retrieval

Retrieval = milliseconds

Archive is rarely accessed · but when needed, get it immediately

Minimum storage = 90 days

6.2. Glacier Flexible Retrieval

Retrieval can choose: Expedited = 1–5 minutes

Standard = 3–5 hours

Bulk = 5–12 hours

Archive is appropriate when: No need to get it right away

6.3. Glacier Deep Archive

Cheapest + retrieval is the slowest

Standard ≈ 12 hours

Bulk ≈ 48 hours

Minimum storage = 180 days

Compliance archive · Long-term backup · Data for many years is rarely accessed

7. Glacier remembers quickly

Instant
= milliseconds

Flexible
= minutes → hours

Deep Archive
= many hours
= cheapest

Minimum storage according to slide: Instant = 90 days

Flexible = 90 days

Deep Archive = 180 days

8. S3 Express One Zone

Express One Zone = high-performance storage class = single AZ

Object is saved in: Directory Bucket

Characteristics: Very low Latency · High request throughput · Compute + storage can colocate in the same AZ

AI / ML · HPC · Financial modeling · Media processing · Latency-sensitive workloads

Points to remember: Express One Zone = performance

One Zone-IA = save costs for rarely accessed data

Both are: Single AZ

but use cases are different.

9. Quick comparison

Storage ClassAccessAZRetrievalMin Storage according to the slide
StandardFrequentMulti-AZImmediateNo
Standard-IAInfrequentMulti-AZImmediate30 days
One Zone-IAInfrequent1 AZImmediate30 days
Intelligent-TieringDon't know the patternMulti-AZAccording to tierDepends on tier
Glacier InstantArchiveMulti-AZMilliseconds90 days
Glacier FlexibleArchiveMulti-AZMinutes → hours90 days

10. S3 Lifecycle Rules

S3 Lifecycle and Amazon DLM

MechanismWhat data management?Function
S3 Lifecycle RulesS3 objects / object versionsTransition storage class, expiration and cleaning multipart uploads according to rules
Amazon Data Lifecycle Manager (DLM)EBS snapshots / EBS-backed AMIsAutomatically create, retain and delete scheduled backups; See EBS snapshots section

DLM manages EBS backups, not the lifecycle of S3 objects, even though EBS snapshots are stored by AWS in S3 on the service side.

Automatic Lifecycle Rules: Transition = change Storage Class

Expiration = delete object

S3 Standard
↓ 60 days
Standard-IA
↓ 180 days
Glacier
↓ 365 days
Delete

→ optimize costs without manual operations.

11. Transition Actions

Used to transfer objects to another storage class.

New Object
↓
Standard

after 30 days
↓
Standard-IA

after 180 days
↓
Glacier

Can apply to: Current Version · Noncurrent Version

Not all storage classes arbitrarily transition to each other; must comply with S3's Lifecycle transition rules.

12. Expiration Actions

Lifecycle can self-delete: Current object · Old object versions · Delete markers · Incomplete multipart uploads

→ Avoid paying for data that is no longer needed.

Prefix · Tags

or: Tag · Department = Finance

→ Minimum duration may still be charged.

Similar: Glacier = retrieval fee + minimum storage duration

Should consider: Storage cost + Retrieval cost + Minimum duration

18. Glacier Expedited Retrieval & Provisioned Capacity Details

Glacier Flexible Recovery CaseTime specification & capacity guarantee
Expedited (< 250 MB)Usually completed in 1–5 minutes when capacity is available
Large objects (from 250 MB to many GB)Don't guarantee completion in 5 minutes even if Expedited is selected
Provisioned CapacityBuy in advance to ensure Expedited retrievals are immediately available for sensitive emergency loads
Quick review · SAA-C03

Select S3 class according to access frequency, retrieval time, AZ number and minimum storage duration; unpredictable access pattern → Intelligent-Tiering.

S3 events & service integrations

Purpose: Automatically handle new or changed objects, instead of constantly checking the bucket.

1. Event stream

Upload object → S3 event → SQS / SNS / Lambda / EventBridge
                           └─ processor reads object from bucket

Event describes the bucket/key/version and operation, not the entire file content. Prefix/suffix can be filtered to only process uploads/ or .jpg.

2. Select destination

SQS serves as a buffer for workers; SNS fan-out; Lambda processing directly; EventBridge has rules/filters and many target types.

The direct SQS/SNS/Lambda destination must be in the same Region as the bucket according to the supported configuration.

S3 does not send directly to the SQS FIFO; Use EventBridge if you need a FIFO target. AWS notification destinations.

3. Where is Permission located?

Destination resource policy for s3.amazonaws.com permissions sqs:SendMessage, sns:Publish or lambda:InvokeFunction.

Limited by source bucket ARN and source account; The purpose of using KMS also requires appropriate key permission.

The consumer role is another layer: the worker/Lambda needs permission to read the object and decrypt it if necessary. Policy for S3 to broadcast events does not automatically grant file reading permissions to consumers.

4. Duplicate events and loops

Event can be delivered more than once and the global order is not guaranteed. The consumer must be idempotent: reprocessing the same object/version does not create unwanted duplicate effects.

Separate input/output prefix or bucket. Lambdas that write thumbnails to the same prefix that are triggering can call themselves indefinitely.

5. Use cases

Use cases: Upload photo → thumbnail. New file → SQS → worker processing with retry. Event by object type → EventBridge → Step Functions for multiple steps.

Quick review · SAA-C03

S3 Event Notifications and EventBridge/SNS/SQS/Lambda for asynchronous processing; Need to understand retry capabilities and how to handle duplicate events.

S3 performance, Requester Pays & Batch Operations

Purpose: Speed up file transfers, manipulate multiple objects, and choose which side pays the cost of reading data.

Tools according to your needs:

Multipart Upload = split file into multiple parts
Byte-range GET = read part of object
Transfer Acceleration = transfer acceleration across the edge
Batch Operations = process multiple objects
Requester Pays = requester pays request/data transfer fee

1. Request rate by prefix

S3 supports at least 3,500 PUT/COPY/POST/DELETE and 5,500 GET/HEAD per second per partitioned prefix.

Distributing workload across multiple prefixes helps increase total throughput; When scaling quickly, you may encounter 503 Slow Down and need to retry/backoff.

Prefix: the beginning of the object key, not the physical folder.

With SSE-KMS, the KMS request quota must also be considered.

2. Multipart Upload

Split object into parts to upload in parallel; If the part is defective, just send that part back.
Usually worth using from about 100 MB, required when exceeding the 5 GB single PUT limit.

The maximum object is now 50 TB via multipart, instead of 5 TB in the old document.

Configure lifecycle cleaning incomplete multipart upload to avoid unfinished part fees. AWS object upload limits.

3. Transfer Acceleration and byte-range GET

Transfer Acceleration receives data at the edge location and then uses the AWS network to reach the bucket, allowing uploading from remote locations via the Internet; must turn on the bucket and measure the benefits versus costs.

Multipart combinations are possible.

Byte-range GET takes part of an object or multiple ranges in parallel, reading header/file chunks and speeding up download; error just retry the corresponding part.

4. Batch Operations

A job includes a manifest object, action, and IAM role.

Used to copy, change tags, restore archive or invoke Lambda on many objects; service tracks and generates completion reports.

Use Inventory/Athena to select objects into the manifest when appropriate. Changing the old encryption object usually requires copy/rewrite, not just changing the default encryption in the bucket.

5. Requester Pays

Authenticated requesters pay request and download fees; The bucket owner still pays for storage. Requester must confirm payment; anonymous access is not supported.

This is a fee mechanism and does not grant permission to read the bucket. Still need policy to allow requests.

6. Use cases

Use cases: Large video: multipart. Upload from multiple continents: try Transfer Acceleration. Retag millions of objects: Batch Operations. Divide large dataset to another account: Requester Pays.

7. Parallel download using S3 Byte-Range Fetches

S3 Large Object
  ├─ HTTP Range: bytes=0-99MB → Worker 1
  ├─ HTTP Range: bytes=100-199MB → Worker 2
  └─ HTTP Range: bytes=200-299MB → Worker 3
  ↓ Merge order at client → Increase download throughput & retry only error parts
Quick review · SAA-C03

Increase S3 performance by designing appropriate requests/transfers; Batch Operations deal with batches of objects, not single direct API reads.

Purpose: Know who is accessing it, how much data is being used and where costs can be reduced or protection increased.

Choose tools according to the question:

Server Access Logging = request to bucket

Storage Lens = usage statistics / configuration
Storage Class Analysis = suggested lifecycle

CloudTrail management events do not replace object data events; You need to enable the appropriate data event type to audit object operations and consider costs.
