11. Use cases

Use cases:

  • Internal applications can withstand long downtime: cross-Region backup and use CloudFormation + AMI to rebuild when needed.
  • Enterprise app needs to reduce DR costs but keep DB persistent: Pilot Light; need an app that is always available on a small scale: Warm Standby.
  • Service with high continuity requirements: production stacks in two Regions, Route 53 and Aurora Global Database with tested DB failover process.
Quick review · SAA-C03

RPO = how much data loss is acceptable; RTO = how quickly recovery is needed. Backup/restore to multi-site active-active increases speed and cost.

AWS Elastic Disaster Recovery (DRS)

Purpose: Restore server from On-Prem / VM / other cloud to AWS when there is a disaster.

1. AWS Elastic Disaster Recovery (DRS)

Old name: CloudEndure Disaster Recovery

Key points:

Continuous replication
+
Low-cost staging
+
Disaster → launch Recovery EC2

2. How DRS works

Install AWS Replication Agent on the source server.

Agent replication changes at level disk block, should protect:

  • OS
  • Application
  • Database
  • Files
Source Server
↓
AWS Replication Agent
↓
Continuous Block Replication
↓
AWS Staging Area
↓ disaster
Recovery EC2

DRS replicates almost both server/disk, not just database.

3. Staging Area

Normally AWS only keeps a low-cost recovery environment:

Source
↓ replication
Staging Area
├─ small replication resources
└─ EBS replica

Khi disaster:

Staging Data
↓
Launch Recovery
↓
Production EC2 + EBS

→ No need to run strong EC2 production 24/7, thus reducing costs.

Staging = a place to keep replicas for recovery, not to serve users.

DRS keeps server replicas in AWS; There was a problem and I turned on EC2 production for recovery.

4. RPO and RTO

Illustration source: Continuous replication → RPO at seconds

Failover → Minute level RTO

But actually depends:

  • network bandwidth
  • replication backlog
  • workload
  • application launch/test time

If the source writes data faster than the replication rate:

Replication Backlog ↑
↓
Actual RPO is worse

5. Failover and Failback

5.1. Failover

When the source site encounters a disaster:

Source ❌
↓
DRS Recovery Point
↓
Launch EC2/EBS
↓
Check Application
↓
Switch Traffic

5.2. Failback

When the source site is back up and running:

AWS Recovery Environment
↓
Failback
↓
Original Site

DRS supports both failover to AWS and failback to source.

6. Database and Application

DRS replicate disk block should be able to protect servers running:

  • Oracle
  • MySQL
  • SQL Server
  • SAP
  • enterprise applications

DRS does not automatically convert the database engine.

For example:

Oracle Source
↓ DRS
EC2 Recovery
↓
still requires an Oracle-compatible environment

7. Ransomware Recovery

Continuous replication: ≠ always have a clean copy.

If ransomware modifies data:

Source is encrypted ❌
↓ replication
Latest Copy can also be encrypted ❌

When recovering, you must choose: recovery point before ransomware occurs

Should still have:

  • backup retention
  • isolated backup
  • recovery point history
  • recovery testing

Replication ≠ Backup.

8. Recovery Environment still needs proper configuration

Recovery EC2 needs to be correct:

  • subnet
  • Security Group
  • instance size
  • dependencies
  • IAM permissions
  • KMS permissions

After launch, check:

EC2 Start
↓
Application Start
↓
DB Consistency
↓
Network Connectivity
↓
Switch Traffic

There is a recovery server: Does not mean the application is ready to serve users.

9. DRS vs Application Migration Service

The two services have quite similar flows:

Source
↓ Agent
Staging
↓
EC2 Target

But the purposes are different:

ServicePurpose
Elastic Disaster Recovery (DRS)DR, failover during disaster, can failback
Application Migration Service (MGN)Migration workload sang AWS

Remember: DRS = long-term protection + recovery in case of failure

MGN = migrate workload sang AWS

10. Use Cases

Use cases:

10.1. On-Prem DR

On-Prem SAP / DB Server
↓ continuous replication
AWS Staging
↓ disaster
Recovery EC2

10.2. Cloud-to-AWS DR

Other Cloud
↓ DRS
AWS

10.3. Ransomware Recovery

Latest Data ❌
↓
Choose Clean Recovery Point
↓
Launch Recovery EC2
↓
Validate
Quick review · SAA-C03

DRS continuously replicates servers for disaster recovery; Different from AWS Backup is copy protection by policy.

AWS DMS & SCT: database migrations

Purpose: Migrate database to AWS with low downtime.

DMS = DATA transfer · SCT = SCHEMA/CODE transfer when changing engine

For example:

Oracle
↓ SCT: convert schema
↓ DMS: migrate data
RDS MySQL

1. AWS Database Migration Service (DMS)

DMS transfers data between databases.

Source DB
↓
DMS Replication Instance
↓
Target DB

Main ingredients of DMS Provisioned:

  • Source Endpoint
  • Target Endpoint
  • Replication Task
  • Replication Instance

Can migrate:

On-Prem ↔ AWS
AWS → AWS

if source/target is supported.

DMS Provisioned and DMS Serverless

How to deployCapacity management
DMS ProvisionedYou choose Replication Instance (CPU/RAM) and actively change the instance class when you need to change capacity.
DMS ServerlessAWS self-provisions and adjusts replication capacity according to workload; No need to manage Replication Instance yourself.

Serverless uses source/target endpoints and replication configuration; You set the capacity limit using DCU and still have to configure network, DB credentials, migration settings. Endpoint/feature must be supported by Serverless.

2. Full Load + CDC

2.1. Full Load

Copy existing data:

Source
↓
Existing Data
↓
Target

2.2. CDC — Change Data Capture

After Full Load, continue replicating:

  • INSERT
  • UPDATE
  • DELETE
Source is still running
↓
Full Load
↓
Target has initial data
↓
CDC
↓
Target catches up with Source
↓
Cutover

Full Load = initial data. CDC = new changes.

→ helps reduce downtime.

3. Homogeneous vs Heterogeneous

3.1. Homogeneous

Same database engine:

PostgreSQL
↓ DMS
RDS PostgreSQL

→ usually just DMS.

3.2. Heterogeneous

Change engine:

Oracle
↓
MySQL

Need:

SCT → convert schema
DMS → migrate data

Same engine → DMS
Different engine → SCT + DMS

4. AWS SCT

SCT convert schema/database code when changing engine.

Can convert:

  • tables
  • views
  • schema
  • stored procedures/code when compatible
Oracle Schema
↓ SCT
MySQL Schema

Some engine-specific code may be required manual fix.

5. Migration Flow

For example Oracle On-Prem → RDS MySQL:

Oracle
├─ SCT → Convert Schema
│
└─ DMS
    ↓
Full Load + CDC
    ↓
RDS MySQL

Before cutover, make sure: The CDC has caught up + valid data/schema

6. DMS Multi-AZ

Multi-AZ provides HA for DMS Replication Instance.

Primary Replication Instance
↓ fail
Standby Instance

Multi-AZ = HA ≠ double throughput ≠ make Source/Target DB Multi-AZ

7. Network & Security

DMS must be able to connect: Source DB · ↕ · DMS · ↕ · Target DB

You may need:

  • VPN / Direct Connect
  • Route Table
  • Security Group
  • DB port
  • DB credentials

IAM permission to manage DMS ≠ database access permission.

8. RDS → Aurora

Can be used:

8.1. Snapshot

RDS Snapshot
↓
Aurora

Simple but doesn't replicate new writes.

8.2. Aurora Read Replica

RDS
↓ replication
Aurora Replica
↓
Promote

8.3. DMS

RDS
↓ Full Load + CDC
Aurora

→ suitable when desired low downtime.

9. Other DMS Targets

DMS is not just DB → DB.

Can target:

  • S3
  • Redshift
  • DynamoDB
  • OpenSearch
  • Kinesis
  • Kafka
  • DocumentDB
Database
↓ DMS
S3 / Redshift

10. External DB → Aurora

If the source is still active and needs to synchronize changes:

External DB
↓
DMS + CDC
↓
Aurora

MySQL can also be migrated using backup/import like:

XtraBackup / mysqldump
↓
Aurora MySQL

11. Use cases

Use cases:

  • Oracle on-premises → RDS MySQL: SCT switches schema, DMS full load + CDC transfers data; cutover after the target catches up.
  • RDS MySQL/PostgreSQL → Aurora: choose snapshot when accepting restore from recovery point, replica when wanting to catch up with writes before promoting.
  • External MySQL → Aurora using Percona XtraBackup/S3 for large initial load; Use DMS when you need to migrate while the database is still active.
  • Replicate operational database to S3, Redshift or streaming target for analytics when the endpoint pair and data shape match.
Quick review · SAA-C03

DMS transfers data with Full Load + CDC to reduce downtime; SCT supports schema/engine conversion when needed.

AWS Backup & Backup Vault Lock

Purpose: Manage and automate backups for multiple AWS services using a centralized policy.

Resources → Backup Plan → Backup Jobs → Backup Vault → Recovery Points

1. What does AWS Backup support?

  • Scheduled/on-demand backup: Backup scheduled or run immediately; Select resource by ID or tag.
  • Retention and lifecycle: Specify how long to keep backups and when to transfer to cold storage if the resource supports it.
  • Common resources: EC2/EBS, RDS/Aurora, S3, DynamoDB, EFS, FSx, DocumentDB/Neptune. PITR, cold storage and copy capabilities vary by service.

2. Plan, Vault and Recovery Point

  • Backup Plan = policy. Configure frequency, backup window, lifecycle, retention and resource assignment.
  • Backup Vault = place to manage backups. Recovery Point = version that can be used to restore.
  • Backing up more frequently creates recovery points closer together, helping to reduce RPO; still need to test restore to check RTO and recovery ability.
Plan: every 12 hours · keep for 30 days · apply tag Backup=Daily
↓
Selected Resources → Recovery Points in Vault

3. Copy to Region or another account

  • Cross-Region: Copy backup to another Region to recover when the source Region has problems.
  • Cross-account: Copy to backup account to increase isolation when production account is compromised.
  • Need correct vault policy, IAM/service role and KMS permissions; Check if the resource supports that type of copy.

4. PITR

Point-In-Time Recovery = restore to a specific point in time within the supported range. Only applies to supported services, not available for all resources.

10:36: data is still good
↓
10:37: error occurred
↓
PITR returns 10:36 if in recovery window

5. Backup Vault Lock

WORM = Write Once Read Many. Vault Lock protects recovery points from unauthorized deletion or retention changes.

  • Governance mode: People with appropriate permissions can still change/remove the lock.
  • Compliance mode: After the grace period, the lock cannot be changed/removed; Even root does not bypass retention.
  • Lock does not prove a clean backup. Data that has been damaged by ransomware can still be backed up and locked; Needs lots of recovery points, appropriate retention and restore testing.

6. Use cases

Use cases:

  • Tag EC2/EBS/RDS to create a backup management plan for the entire environment.
  • DR: keep a copy in another Region; Increase isolation with a separate backup account.
  • Compliance/ransomware: Vault Lock keeps backups until the end of retention, choosing a recovery point before the incident when restoring.
Quick review · SAA-C03

AWS Backup manages multi-service backup policies; Backup Vault Lock helps protect retention, not replacing restore testing.

AWS Application Discovery Service & Application Migration Service (MGN)

Purpose: Survey the current system → monitor migration → rehost server to EC2.

Discovery Service
↓
Migration Hub
↓
MGN
↓
EC2

1. Application Discovery Service

Purpose: Learn the system before migrating.

Collect:

  • number of servers
  • CPU / RAM / Disk
  • utilization
  • server dependencies

Help: Right-size EC2 + divide migration waves + know which servers depend on each other

1.1. Agentless

Do not install agents on each server.

Agentless
→ inventory + utilization
→ suitable for quick survey of many VMs

1.2. Agent-based

Install Discovery Agent on the server.

Collect more: Processes + Network connections + Dependencies

For example:

Web
↓
App
↓
DB

Agentless = general. Agent-based = more detailed dependencies.

2. Migration Hub

Migration Hub = centralized place to view and track migrations.

Discovery Data
↓
Migration Hub
↓
Track applications / servers / progress

Used for:

  • xem discovery data
  • Group servers into applications
  • track migration progress

Migration Hub is not a tool to directly migrate servers.

3. Application Migration Service (MGN)

MGN = rehost / lift-and-shift server to EC2.

Support:

Physical Server
VM
Other Cloud
↓
AWS EC2

MGN installed AWS Replication Agent on source.

Source Server
↓
Replication Agent
↓
Continuous Replication
↓
Low-cost Staging
↓
Test
↓
Cutover
↓
EC2

Source can still run while replicating → reduce downtime.

4. Test & Cutover

4.1. Test

Launch EC2 test to test:

  • server boot
  • application
  • network
  • dependencies

4.2. Cutover

Once the target has caught up:

Source
↓
Final replication
↓
Launch Production EC2
↓
Validate
↓
Switch traffic

Minimal downtime ≠ zero downtime.

5. MGN = Rehost

MGN mainly keeps the same server:

On-Prem Server
↓ MGN
EC2 Server

MGN not self:

Oracle → Aurora ❌
Monolith → Lambda ❌

Change database engine: DMS + SCT

6. MGN vs DRS

The two services have quite similar flows:

Source
↓ Agent
Staging
↓
EC2

But another purpose: MGN = Migration = move server to AWS

DRS = Disaster Recovery = keep the replica for recovery in case of failure

7. App2Container and AWS Transform

ServiceConcept
AWS App2Container (A2C)CLI tool analyzes legacy applications on servers and dependencies, creates Docker/container images and deployment artifacts for ECS/EKS; Supports compatible applications.
AWS TransformThe service uses AI agents to analyze, migrate and modernize applications, code, dependencies and infrastructure according to supported workloads.
App2Container
Legacy Application on the server
↓ containerize
Docker Containers → ECS/EKS

AWS Transform
Legacy Application / Infrastructure
↓ AI agents analysis + conversion
Modernized Solution on AWS

A2C focuses on containerization; Transform has a broader scope of modernization. MGN maintains the direction of rehosting the server to EC2; Don't turn monoliths into microservices yourself.

Service update: App2Container stopped accepting new customers from 07/11/2025; Existing customers can complete ongoing projects. AWS recommends Transform for new projects. ·

8. Use cases

Use cases:

  • Inventory and dependency mapping for data center before dividing migration waves, choosing instance size and transfer order of application groups.
  • Rehost a legacy enterprise app from VMware/physical servers or another cloud to EC2 while keeping the OS/application stack.
  • Use Migration Hub to monitor multiple waves; Use MGN for servers and DMS/SCT for DB engine conversion in the same migration program.

9. Compare options

ServiceRemember
Discovery ServiceSurvey server + dependencies
Migration HubTrack migrations
MGNRehost server → EC2
DMS + SCTMigrate database
DRSDisaster Recovery
App2ContainerLegacy application → Docker container
AWS TransformLegacy application / infrastructure → modernized solution using AI

10. Agentless vs Agent-based Discovery & Migration Hub Home Region

ConceptDistinguish
Agentless vs Agent-based DiscoveryAgentless deploys quickly via VMware vCenter connector (CPU/RAM/disk); Agent-based installed on each physical server/VM to collect in-depth process and network dependencies
Migration Hub Home Region vs Destination RegionHome Region just a central place to store discovery metadata and migration progress status; The actual server/database still moves directly to any location Destination Region Which destination?
Quick review · SAA-C03

Application Discovery helps survey workloads/dependencies; MGN moves servers to AWS using replication and cutover.

Hybrid migration: VM Import/Export, bulk transfer & VMware Cloud on AWS

Purpose: Choose how to migrate VM / large amounts of data / VMware workloads from On-Prem to AWS.

VM image → VM Import/Export
Bulk large data → Snowball
VMware workload → VMware Cloud on AWS

1. VM Import/Export

Purpose: Import existing VM to EC2 or export EC2 VM to On-Prem.

1.1. Import

VM Image
↓
S3
↓
VM Import/Export
↓
AMI
↓
EC2

Can be used to:

  • migrate VM to EC2
  • Save VM images for recovery/DR
  • test workload on AWS

1.2. Export

EC2
↓
Export VM Image
↓
On-Prem

Only supported when OS / image / licensing / format suitable.

VM Import/Export = migration by image. MGN = continuous replication of the running server.

2. Amazon Linux VM Image

Amazon Linux can run On-Prem on hypervisors such as:

  • VMware
  • KVM
  • VirtualBox
  • Hyper-V

Note:

VM image is not always the case .iso; format depends on hypervisor.

3. Bulk Data Transfer

When transferring very large datasets, you need to consider:

Data Size + Bandwidth + Setup Time

For example 200 TB:

WayMain idea
Internet/VPN 100 Mbps~185 ideal days
Direct Connect 1 Gbps~18.5 days of transmission, not including setup time
SnowballMay be faster for initial bulk load

This is an illustrative example only, not an SLA.

4. Snowball

Snowball = transferring large amounts of data using a physical device.

On-Prem Data
↓
Copy to Snowball
↓
Ship device
↓
AWS imports data

Suitable when:

Dataset is very large
+
WAN is slow
→ Transfer over network takes too long

Snowball mainly deals initial bulk load, not continuous replication.

5. Initial Load + Ongoing Changes

Can combine:

Initial Data
↓
Snowball
↓
AWS

New Changes
↓
VPN/Direct Connect
↓
DMS or DataSync
Database changes
→ DMS + CDC

Files / Storage
→ DataSync

After the target catches up:

Source
↓
Final sync
↓
Cutover
↓
AWS

6. VMware Cloud on AWS

Purpose: Run VMware workloads on AWS but keep the VMware stack.

On-Prem VMware
↓
VMware Cloud on AWS
├─ vSphere
├─ vSAN
└─ NSX
↓
AWS Services

6.1. Ingredients

VMware Stack componentsFunction
vSphere / vCenterRun hypervisor and centrally manage VM virtual machines
vSANSoftware-defined storage
NSXNetwork virtualization and security (Networking & Security)
  • using big VMware
  • Want to migrate/expand data center to AWS
  • still want to use vCenter/vSphere
  • Don't want to move workload to native EC2 yet

7. VMware Cloud vs MGN

7.1. VMware Cloud

VMware On-Prem
↓
VMware Cloud on AWS

→ keep VMware environment

7.2. MGN

Server / VM
↓
MGN
↓
Native EC2

→ rehost sang AWS EC2

VMware Cloud = keep VMware. MGN = switch to native EC2.

8. Use cases

Use cases:

  • Import a supported VM image into EC2 or keep the repository for recovery without a continuous MGN pipeline.
  • Initial load 200 TB using Snowball with only 100 Mbps WAN, then continue updating via DMS/DataSync and VPN/DX.
  • Expand or migrate vSphere estate to AWS when the team needs to continue administration with vCenter/vSphere, and integrate S3, FSx, RDS or analytics services.
Quick review · SAA-C03

VM Import/Export handles virtual machines; Snow Family handles bulk offline transfers; Select by source type and bandwidth.

AWS Backup flashboard — SAA-C03

Purpose: Compare backup and data protection mechanisms to choose the right recovery based on service, timing, and retention.

1. What data to backup?

Service / mechanismDataFunction / when selected
RDS Automated BackupsRDS databaseAutomatic backup + transaction logs; PITR support up to 35 days when enabled
Aurora Automated BackupsAurora clusterContinuous, incremental backup; PITR up to 35 days; always on
RDS / Aurora Manual SnapshotDatabaseCopy at a time, keep until actively deleted
Amazon Data Lifecycle Manager (DLM)EBS volumes / EBS-backed AMIsCreate, keep, and delete scheduled EBS snapshots/AMIs
EBS SnapshotEBS volumeBackup incremental; restore to new EBS volume
AWS BackupEBS, RDS, Aurora, EFS, DynamoDB, FSx, S3…Centralized management: schedule, retention, cross-Region/cross-account copy when resources support
AWS Backup Continuous BackupSupport resources like RDS, Aurora, S3PITR in recovery window is configured; Does not apply to all resources
S3 VersioningS3 objectsKeep multiple versions; Recover when overwritten/wrongly deleted if the required version is still available
S3 Replication (SRR/CRR)S3 objectsCopy to bucket of same Region or different Region
S3 Object LockS3 object versionsWORM: prevent version deletion/editing in retention; governance has appropriate bypass permissions
DynamoDB PITRDynamoDB tableContinuous backup; restore back in time within a maximum of 35 days to a new table
DynamoDB On-Demand BackupDynamoDB tableCreate full backups when needed, keep until deleted
EFS Automatic BackupEFS file systemAWS Backup automatically backs up according to the configured policy

Replication can propagate changes/deletions according to configuration, so backup/version history is still needed to go back to before the error. Object Lock protects versions; Do not create an independent backup yourself.

2. Retention and characteristics

Backup typeRetention / characteristics
RDS AutomatedDB instance: 0–35 days, 0 is off; Multi-AZ DB cluster: 1–35 days
Aurora Automated1–35 days; Automated backups cannot be turned off
DynamoDB PITRRecovery window 1–35 days, default 35 when enabled
RDS / Aurora Manual SnapshotKeep until deleted
EBS SnapshotIncremental; Keep until deleted or processed by lifecycle policy
AWS Backup ContinuousPITR up to 35 days; depending on the configured service and retention
AWS Backup PeriodicRetention according to backup plan; Supports long-term storage, cold storage capabilities depending on resources

AWS Backup continuous supports RDS instances/Aurora accordingly, but does not support RDS Multi-AZ DB clusters. Copy continuous backup to another Region/account to create a snapshot copy, without keeping the PITR window of the source copy.

· · ·

3. Use cases

Use cases:

  • Wrongly deleting database data: PITR forward error if still in recovery window.
  • Backup EBS daily and keep for 7 days: DLM; Many services/accounts need common management: AWS Backup.
  • Save database copies for many months/years: manual snapshot or periodic backup with appropriate retention.
  • S3 overwritten: restore old version; Need to prevent version deletion according to retention: Object Lock with appropriate mode.
Quick review · SAA-C03

Remember to distinguish between backup, replication, archive and DR: each solution has a different purpose, recovery time and scope.

Well-Architected & Trusted Advisor

AWS Well-Architected Framework & Tool

Purpose: AWS Well-Architected Framework helps evaluate architecture according to best practices instead of just looking at individual services. AWS Well-Architected Tool turns that review process into trackable workloads, questions, risks, and improvement plans.

1. 6 pillars

The six pillars are not separate trade-offs; Good design often makes them support each other.

Operational Excellence
= operate, observe, automate and continuously improve

Security
= identity, data, system and access control protection

Reliability
= fault tolerance, recovery and response to changing needs

Performance Efficiency
= use resources of the right type, right size, at the right time

Cost Optimization
= achieve business goals at reasonable costs

Sustainability
= reduce environmental impact by using resources efficiently

Pillars = an architectural testing system, not a checklist to memorize line by line.

2. Workload review

Workload is a set of resources and code that delivers business value, for example a web app, backend process or data pipeline.

Define workload
↓
Select lens
↓
Answer review questions
↓
Identify high / medium risks
↓
Improvement plan

The tool does not edit the architecture itself and does not replace the architect review; it standardizes questions, saves decisions, and points out areas for improvement.

3. AWS Well-Architected Tool

Well-Architected Tool is a free service in AWS Console to review workloads according to 6 pillars and related lenses.

Console
↓
AWS Well-Architected Tool
↓
Workloads
↓
Reports / Dashboard / Improvement plan

Common process: Select workload · Answer questions · Review results by pillar · Generate report · Track improvement status

Dashboard displays total workload and number of high/medium risk issues; Report helps share results with people who are not directly accessing the tool. AWS WA Tool

4. Access and scope

IAM controls who can create, view, edit, or share review workloads. Review answers are architectural information, so do not enter unnecessary secrets, passwords or sensitive data.

The tool mainly works on metadata and review responses; it does not install agents into your workload and does not grant data-plane permissions to your application.

Console/API access using TLS; The power to improve the actual architecture still lies with the target services such as IAM, VPC, RDS, S3 or CloudFormation.

5. Use cases

Use cases:

  • Review a workload before production launch to find high risk issues regarding security, reliability or cost.
  • Track architectural improvements each sprint using improvement plans and milestone/report.
  • Standardize reviews of multiple workloads within an organization, instead of each team using its own checklist.
Quick review · SAA-C03

Well-Architected has architectural design pillars; Tool supports workload assessment and improvement planning.

AWS Trusted Advisor

Purpose: AWS Trusted Advisor audits accounts at a high level and provides recommendations to improve costs, security, performance, fault tolerance, service quotas, and operations.

1. Account assessment

Trusted Advisor does not need to install agents into EC2; It reads the configuration and resource status in the account to create recommendations.

AWS account
↓
Trusted Advisor checks
↓
Recommendations
↓
Review / remediate

For example, checks can detect public snapshots, IAM root access keys, service quotas near limits, or resources that can optimize costs.

Trusted Advisor is a recommendation tool, not a guardrail that blocks incorrect operations at deployment time.

2. Check categories

Common main categories: Cost Optimization · Performance · Security · Fault Tolerance · Service Quotas · Operational Excellence

CategoryArchitectural example
SecurityCheck for risky public or credential configurations.
Fault ToleranceRemind the design to be more fault tolerant, for example backup or Multi-AZ are suitable.
Service QuotasWarning about quota almost running out before scaling gets stuck.
Cost OptimizationFind idle resources or more economical options.

3. Support plan and API

In the console, the level of checks available depends on the support plan. Full check set and permission to use Trusted Advisor/Support API require appropriate support plan such as Business Support+, Enterprise Support or Unified Operations.

Programmatic access is useful when you want to get recommendations for multiple accounts or integrate with internal dashboards. Trusted Advisor API

Trusted Advisor API
↓
List checks / recommendations
↓
Aggregate by account
↓
Remediation workflow

4. Access and security

IAM controls who can see the Trusted Advisor and call the API. With multiple accounts, administrators often aggregate data through AWS Organizations and appropriate cross-account permissions.

Trusted Advisor recommendations may contain resource configuration information, so view permissions should only be granted to those who need to operate them. Console/API access using TLS; True remediation still requires permissions on the target service.

5. Use cases

Use cases:

  • Account review periodically to detect S3/EBS/RDS public exposure, quotas are about to be full or resources are costing money.
  • Security team gathers Trusted Advisor findings from multiple accounts to prioritize remediation.
  • Before scaling a workload, check service quotas and fault-tolerance recommendations to avoid errors when increasing load.
Quick review · SAA-C03

Trusted Advisor provides recommendations on cost, performance, fault tolerance, service limits and security according to supported features.

Governance, Cost & Other Services

AWS CloudFormation

Purpose: Write AWS infrastructure into code to create/update resources automatically, instead of manually configuring on Console.

YAML / JSON Template
↓
CloudFormation
↓
Stack
↓
AWS Resources

Templates and Stack

Template = YAML/JSON file describing the infrastructure to create.

For example:

VPC · EC2 · ALB · RDS

Stack = real resources created by CloudFormation from templates.

The same template can be used for: Dev · Test · Prod

Common parts:

  • Resources = resource to create

  • Parameters = input

  • Outputs = output, for example endpoint

  • Conditions = condition whether to create a resource or not

Change Set and Rollback

Change Set = CloudFormation preview will:

Add · Modify · Delete · Replace

any resource before updating.

Rollback = if update fails, CloudFormation tries to return the stack to the previous state.

Rollback is not a data backup.

DeletionPolicy

  • Retain = delete stack but keep resource

  • Snapshot = create a snapshot before deletion if the resource supports it

StackSets

= deploy the same CloudFormation template to:

Multiple AWS Accounts · + · Multiple Regions

Suitable for AWS Organizations / multi-account.

Drift Detection

Drift = The real resource is edited differently from the template.

For example:

Templates:
SG permit 443

Someone edit Console:
SG allow 22

→ Drift

Drift Detection help detect that difference.

Infrastructure Composer and Service Role

Infrastructure Composer = intuitive interface for designing CloudFormation templates.

Service Role = IAM Role that CloudFormation uses to:

Create · Update · Delete

AWS resources.

Remember quickly

  • CloudFormation = Infrastructure as Code
  • Template = YAML/JSON blueprint
  • Stack = resources are created from template
  • Change Set = preview changes
  • Rollback = update error → return to previous state
  • StackSets = deploy multiple accounts/Region
  • Drift Detection = detected that the resource was edited differently from the template

Memory sentence: Template → CloudFormation → Stack → AWS Resources

Amazon SES & Amazon Pinpoint

Purpose:

  • SES = send/receive email for application
  • Pinpoint = in old documents, used to divide customer groups and run campaigns
SES
= Email service

Pinpoint
= Customer segment + Campaign

Amazon SES

SES = Simple Email Service

Used to send email applications such as:

  • registration confirmation
  • reset password
  • invoice
  • newsletter

Flow:

Application / Lambda
↓
SES API / SMTP
↓
Email
↓
User

SES helps eliminate the need to operate the mail server yourself.

SES, not Gmail/Outlook, has an inbox interface for users.

Verified Identity and Sandbox

If you want to send email using SES, you must verify:

  • email address
  • or domains

Sandbox = initial limit mode.

In the sandbox, you can usually only send to:

  • Verified email/domain
  • mailbox simulator

When available Production Access:

  • No need to verify recipient
  • still have to verify the sender

Sender always needs Verified Identity.

Amazon Pinpoint

In old documentation: Pinpoint = used to:

  • divide customer groups (segments)
  • create campaign (campaigns)
  • create customer journeys
  • engagement tracking

For example:

The customer has not yet made a purchase
↓
Segment
↓
Discount campaign
↓
Email/SMS/Push

Different:

SES
= send email

Pinpoint
= select customer group + run campaign

Pinpoints in this document are mainly important to remember to distinguish roles from SES.

Remember quickly

  • SES = send/receive email for application
  • Verified Identity = verify sender email/domain
  • Sandbox = Production Access pre-limited mode
  • Pinpoint = customer segment + campaign + engagement

Memory sentence: SES = send email, Pinpoint = choose who to send and run the campaign.

AWS Systems Manager

Purpose: Remotely administer EC2 and external AWS servers without having to manually SSH each machine.

Systems Manager
↓
SSM Agent + IAM + Network
↓
Managed Nodes
↓
Session / Command / Patch / Automation

1. Conditions for machine management

Machine needs:

  • SSM Agent

  • IAM permission is appropriate

  • Connect to Systems Manager

EC2 private subnet is still usable, you can pass VPC Endpoint.

No need to public IP or open port 22 to use Systems Manager only.

2. Session Manager

= into the EC2/server shell from Console or CLI.

Admin
↓
Session Manager
↓
Private EC2

Advantages:

  • no need for SSH key

  • No need for Bastion Host

  • No need to open inbound port 22

Session Manager = secure access to the machine without direct SSH.

3. Run Command

= run remote commands on one or more machines.

For example:

100 EC2
↓
Run Command
↓
restart service

Used for:

  • run script

  • restart service

  • install software

Run Command = run the same command on multiple machines.

4. Patch Manager

= check and install operating system patch.

EC2
↓ Scan
Patch Manager
↓
Detect missing patch
↓ Install
Install patches

Patch Baseline = specifies which patches are accepted.

Patch Manager = If your device is missing a patch, check and install that patch.

5. Maintenance Windows

= regulations When can maintenance be run?.

For example:

Sunday
02:00 - 04:00
↓
Patch / Restart / Run Command

Distinguish:

Patch Manager
= Which patch should I install?

Maintenance Window
= when to install

Maintenance Window = maintenance schedule.

6. Automation

Automation Runbook is a multi-step automation script AWS Systems Manager Automation execute in a specified sequence/flow to manage AWS resources.

Instead of the admin manually manipulating each step, the runbook runs the entire process itself; can call AWS API, run script, wait or request approval.

For example:

Create AMI
↓
Stop EC2
↓
Update Config
↓
Start EC2
↓
Verify

Automation = automating a multi-step process.

7. State Manager

= hold on desired configuration.

For example, if you want every EC2 to always have a CloudWatch Agent:

Desired:
CloudWatch Agent must be installed

Actual:
EC2 does not have an Agent yet

↓
State Manager

→ return the device to the desired state

State Manager = ensures the machine's configuration is always as desired.

8. Operating conditions

Automation's Execution role must have permissions for each target service; Not every step requires SSM Agent on the device. Session Manager can logs to S3/CloudWatch Logs when configured, but SSH tunnel/port forwarding content is not logged this way.

Run Command supports concurrency limits/error thresholds; Patch Manager is not used to upgrade major OS versions.

9. Use cases

Use cases: Runbook creates AMI, updates configuration, and verifies EC2; Maintenance Window selects running hours; The State Manager keeps the CloudWatch Agent installed.

Quick review · SAA-C03

Systems Manager manages fleet operations: Run Command executes commands; Patch Manager patches the machine; Session Manager access without opening SSH.

AWS Cost Explorer, Budgets & Cost Anomaly Detection

Purpose: Track expenses, set budgets, and detect unusual spending.

Cost Explorer
= Where was the money spent?

Budgets
= Is the set limit exceeded?

Cost Anomaly Detection
= Are the costs unusual?

1. Cost Explorer

Cost Explorer = view and analyze AWS costs.

You can filter/group by:

  • Service

  • Account

  • Region

  • Tags

For example:

Bill increased
↓
Cost Explorer
↓
EC2 increase?
Data Transfer increased?
S3 increased?

Yes Forecast to predict future costs based on previous data.

Cost Explorer = cost analysis and trends.

2. Savings Plans Recommendations

Cost Explorer can analyze usage and:

Suggested Savings Plans

to reduce costs if the workload is stable.

Yes:

  • Coverage = how much usage is covered by Savings Plan

  • Utilization = how much of the committed portion is used

This is just recommendation, do not purchase the Savings Plan yourself.

3. AWS Budgets

Budgets = set cost/usage thresholds and alert when they are near or exceeded.

For example:

Monthly Budget = $500

Forecast > $500
↓
Send Alert

You can alert according to:

  • Actual cost

  • Forecast cost

Note:

Budgets don't automatically block costs just because you create them.

Configurable Budget Actions to perform some action when a threshold is reached.

4. Cost Anomaly Detection

Cost Anomaly Detection = use machine learning to detect unusual cost increases.

For example:

Normal Data Transfer = $20/day

Today = $150/day
↓
Anomaly Detection
↓
Alert

Other Budget:

Budget
= exceeds the threshold I set?

Anomaly Detection
= unusually different from the normal pattern?

Abnormalities can be detected even when the total cost has not exceeded the budget.

5. AWS Compute Optimizer

AWS Compute Optimizer Analyze configuration and utilization metrics to recommend rightsizing, avoid resource overprovisioning, or improve performance. Need to opt in and have enough metrics data.

EC2 m5.2xlarge is rarely used
↓ CPU / memory if available / network / IOPS / throughput
Compute Optimizer
↓ Illustrative recommendation: m5.large if it meets the workload
Review peak load and application requirements
↓
Change configuration to reduce costs

An average CPU of 10% is not enough to confirm that a specific instance type should be changed. Compute Optimizer mainly giving suggestions, do not default to self-resize EC2/EBS.

ResourceRecommended
EC2Instance type/size is appropriate
Auto Scaling GroupRightsizing compute by ASG
EBSVolume type, IOPS, throughput
LambdaMemory is suitable
ECS FargateCPU and memory
RDS / AuroraConfigure the database appropriately
DynamoDB, NAT Gateway, ElastiCacheOptimized according to the capabilities and conditions of each supported resource

EC2 memory metrics must be provided via an agent or supported source.

6. Compare tools

ServiceFunction
CloudWatchCollect metrics/logs, monitor performance and alarms
Compute OptimizerAnalyze metrics/configuration and recommend resource optimization
Cost ExplorerAnalyze costs/usage and trends
BudgetsActual/forecast alerts according to set thresholds
Cost Anomaly DetectionDetect costs that are unusual compared to the pattern
Data Lifecycle ManagerAutomatically manage scheduled EBS snapshots / EBS-backed AMIs

Billing data has latency. Forecasts are forecasts, not guaranteed prices; See upcoming needs before purchasing Savings Plans. Budget Actions need their own IAM role/permissions; Permission to view invoices does not automatically grant permission to stop EC2.

7. Use cases

Use cases: If the bill increases, use Cost Explorer to find the service/account causing the increase; If you want to warn about budget overruns, use Budgets; If you want to rightsize EC2/EBS according to usage, see Compute Optimizer and check your workload before applying.

8. Cost Management Ecosystem, Budgets Actions, Savings Plans & Reserved Capacity

Billing & Cost ToolsSpecialized roles
Cost and Usage Report (CUR) / AWS Data ExportsExport the most detailed cost & usage reports (by hour/resource/tag) to S3 for analysis Athena or QuickSight
Cost Allocation Tags & Cost CategoriesTags label cost allocation by project/department; Cost Categories group costs according to a set of logical rules
AWS Pricing CalculatorEstimate architectural costs before deployment
AWS Billing ConductorCreate custom price lists (proforma billing, plus separate markup/discounts) for service Chargeback / Showback internally without changing the original AWS invoice collected at the Management Account
Cost Optimization HubCentralize, de-duplicate and prioritize all savings proposals (idle, rightsizing, RI/SP) across multiple Accounts/Regions
Instance Scheduler on AWSThe available CloudFormation solution automatically turns on/off EC2 and RDS according to the tagging schedule (for example, only running Dev/Test environment office hours)

AWS Budgets Actions: Goes beyond standard email alerts — when a budget threshold is reached, it will automatically (or wait for approval) be executed Apply IAM Policy, Apply SCP, or Stop EC2 / RDS Instances specify.

4 Types of AWS Savings PlansFlexible rangeTerm
Compute Savings PlansMost flexible: self-applied throughout EC2, AWS Fargate, and AWS Lambda (Can change Region, instance family, OS, tenancy)1 or 3 years
EC2 Instance Savings PlansDeeper drop on EC2 but fixed one Instance Family + Region (can still change AZ, size, OS)1 or 3 years
SageMaker AI Savings PlansApplies to eligible SageMaker AI compute components1 or 3 years
Database Savings PlansHourly commitments for qualified DB services (Aurora Serverless v2, next generation RDS/Aurora support, DynamoDB, ElastiCache, OpenSearch, DMS…)1 year

Reserved Instances / Reserved Capacity (1 or 3 years): Apply separately for each service such as EC2 RI, RDS/Aurora Reserved DB Instances, DynamoDB Reserved Capacity (RCU/WCU), ElastiCache/MemoryDB/Redshift Reserved Nodes and Redshift Serverless Reservations (RPU). In Organizations, Consolidated Billing shares Discount offers RI/SP between member accounts but does not share Zonal RI capacity reservation rights under this billing mechanism.

Quick review · SAA-C03

Cost Explorer analyzes costs; Budgets tracks preset thresholds; Cost Anomaly Detection detects unusual costs.

AWS Outposts

Purpose: Bringing AWS infrastructure to the business's datacenter to run workloads on-site but still using the familiar management method of AWS.

AWS Region
↕ Service Link
AWS Outposts at Datacenter
↓
Local Workloads

What is AWS Outposts?

Outposts = AWS hardware is set right at your datacenter.

Can run some supported AWS resources on-prem.

Suitable when needed:

  • low latency with local system

  • Process/save data at specific location

  • still want to use AWS tools/API

Not all AWS services can run on Outposts.

Connect to AWS Region

Outposts still connect back AWS Region cha via:

Service Link

Outposts
↓ Service Link
AWS Region

Service Link is used for traffic management and connection to AWS Region.

Outposts is not a standalone AWS cloud that is completely offline.

Responsibility

AWS = Outposts infrastructure management.

Enterprise = prepare:

  • datacenter

  • electricity

  • cooling

  • network

  • application/data

IAM, Security Group and encryption still need normal configuration.

Outposts vs Direct Connect vs Storage Gateway

ServiceWhat to use?
OutpostsRun AWS compute/resources right at the datacenter
Direct ConnectPrivate network line from on-prem to AWS
Storage GatewayConnect on-prem storage to AWS storage

Remember:

Outposts
= AWS runs at On-Prem

Direct Connect
= On-Prem connection ↔ AWS

Storage Gateway
= connect Storage On-Prem ↔ AWS

Remember quickly

  • AWS Outposts = bring AWS infrastructure to datacenter
  • Main use case = low latency + requires data/local processing
  • Service Link = connect Outposts to AWS Region
  • Outposts ≠ Direct Connect = Outposts have on-premises compute; DX is just a network line

Memory sentence: Outposts = AWS infrastructure runs right in your datacenter.

AWS Batch

Purpose: Run large batch jobs without having to manage the server/scheduler yourself.

Job Definition
↓
Submit Job
↓
Job Queue
↓
Scheduler
↓
Compute Environment
↓
Container Job

Main ingredients

Job Definition = configure how the job will run:

  • Container image
  • Command
  • CPU / RAM
  • IAM Role
  • Job = a particular run.
  • Job Queue = queue contains jobs that have not yet run.
  • Compute Environment = resources used to run the job, usually:
  • EC2
  • Fargate
  • EC2 Spot if the job can withstand interruption

For example:

1000 images need to be processed
↓
Submit 1000 Jobs
↓
Job Queue
↓
AWS Batch provides compute on its own
↓
Containers handled
↓
Output → S3

AWS Batch = Queue + Scheduler + Compute for batch jobs.

Batch vs Lambda vs Fargate

  • AWS Batch = coordinate multiple batch jobs, suitable for heavy/long-running jobs.
  • Lambda = event-driven function, suitable for short tasks; Maximum normal lambda 15 minutes.
  • Fargate = where containers run without server management; can do compute under AWS Batch.
Batch
= job management / queue / scheduling

Fargate
= provides compute for the container to run

IAM

Job Role = permissions of the code inside the container.

For example:

Batch Job
↓ Job Role
S3 Read / Write

Permission to submit Batch job does not mean the container itself has access to S3.

Remember quickly

  • AWS Batch = run and coordinate batch jobs
  • Job Definition = how job runs
  • Job Queue = job is waiting
  • Compute Environment = EC2/Fargate is used to run jobs
  • Spot = saves jobs that can withstand interruptions

Memory sentence: Job Definition → Queue → Compute → Container runs.

Multi-Node Parallel (MNP) & Batch Scheduling Policy vs EventBridge Scheduler

Mechanism on AWS BatchFunctions & Limitations to remember
Multi-Node Parallel (MNP)Allows a large HPC/ML job to run simultaneously on multiple EC2 nodes coordinating via MPI (main node coordinates child nodes); Not supported on Fargate
AWS Batch Scheduling PolicyFair-share and prioritize resources among job groups in the Job Queue (no cron timer)
Amazon EventBridge SchedulerDecision time Trigger a job to run periodically (e.g. every day at 8:00 a.m. send the job to AWS Batch or ECS)
Amazon AppFlow

Purpose: Transfer data between SaaS applications and AWS services without writing a lot of integration code yourself.

SaaS / AWS Source
↓
AppFlow
↓ filter / map / transform
Destination

For example:

Salesforce
↓
AppFlow
↓
S3 / Redshift

Source and Destination

AppFlow uses connector to connect source/destination.

Possible:

  • map fields

  • filter data

  • validation

  • transform is simple

Not all connectors support all transmission directions.

AppFlow = managed data flow between SaaS ↔ AWS.

When does Flow run?

  • On Demand = runs when the user calls
  • Schedule = run periodically
  • Event = runs when source has change event and connector support

For example:

Salesforce update
↓
AppFlow
↓
S3

Scheduled flows can:

  • Full = get all data

  • Incremental = only get new/changed data

Security

AppFlow supports:

  • encryption in transit

  • encryption at rest

  • IAM

  • KMS

  • SaaS credentials / OAuth

If destination is S3/Redshift, appropriate permissions are still needed.

PrivateLink only applies when the connector supports it.

AppFlow vs Glue vs DMS

  • AppFlow = Simple SaaS integration
  • Glue = ETL / transform data is more complicated
  • DMS = migrate/replicate database

For example:

Salesforce
↓ AppFlow
S3
↓ Glue
Transform Data

Remember quickly

  • AppFlow = SaaS ↔ AWS data transfer
  • Trigger = On Demand / Schedule / Event
  • Incremental = only get new/changed data
  • Glue = deeper processing/ETL
  • DMS = database migration/replication

Memory sentence: AppFlow = connects SaaS to AWS to transfer data using managed flows.

AWS Amplify

Purpose: Help developers build and deploy web/mobile apps faster, including frontend hosting and AWS backend connection.

Git Repository
↓
Amplify Build / Deploy
↓
Hosted Frontend
↓
Auth / Data / Storage / Functions

Amplify Hosting

Amplify Hosting = build + deploy frontend from Git.

For example:

GitHub
↓ push code
Amplify
↓
Build + Deploy
↓
Website

Support:

  • CI/CD

  • HTTPS

  • Custom Domain

  • CDN

  • deploy according to branch

For example:

main
= Production

develop
= Test

Amplify Hosting = frontend hosting + managed CI/CD.

Backend

Amplify helps connect AWS backends such as:

Amplify feature arrayAWS Services is behind
Auth (User login)Amazon Cognito
Data / API (Save & query)AWS AppSync / Amazon DynamoDB
Storage (Save file/media)Amazon S3
Functions (Backend logic)AWS Lambda

Flow:

Frontend
↓
Amplify
↓
Cognito / AppSync / DynamoDB / S3 / Lambda

Amplify is not a single server; it helps connect back-end AWS services.

Amplify vs S3 + CloudFront

SolutionSupport range
AWS AmplifyBuilt-in Git CI/CD frontend hosting (for each branch) + quick connection to serverless backend ecosystem for web/mobile app
S3 + CloudFrontPure static hosting and CDN delivery; Need to assemble your own CI/CD pipeline and backend layer

Memory sentence: Amplify = frontend hosting + CI/CD + AWS backend connection for web/mobile app.

Amazon EventBridge Scheduler

Purpose: Run tasks according to time/schedule without needing to maintain the cron server yourself.

Rate / Cron / One-time
↓
EventBridge Scheduler
↓
Execution Role
↓
Target

Schedule types

Rate = repeats after a period of time.

For example:

rate(5 minutes)

Cron = run on a specific schedule.

For example:

Every day 08:00

One-time = runs exactly once at the set time.

Rate = every time, Cron = any time according to the schedule, One-time = runs once.

Target and Execution Role

Scheduler can call:

  • Lambda
  • SQS
  • Step Functions
  • many other AWS APIs

Flow:

It's time
↓
Scheduler assumes Execution Role
↓
Call Target

Execution Role Must have appropriate permissions such as:

  • invoke Lambda
  • send message SQS
  • start Step Functions

Retry and DLQ

If calling target fails:

Scheduler
↓
Retry
↓ still fails
SQS DLQ

Retry = try again.

DLQ = holds the failure event for later processing.

Target should handle it idempotent because retry can be duplicated.

Scheduler vs EventBridge Event Bus

Scheduler = trigger according time

For example:

Every night 01:00
↓
Run Lambda

EventBridge Event Bus = trigger according event

For example:

EC2 state changed
↓
EventBridge Rule
↓
Lambda

Remember quickly

EventBridge Scheduler = cron/rate/one-time managed service

Rate = repeat at interval

Cron = specific calendar

  • One-time = run once
  • Execution Role = permission for Scheduler to call target
  • Retry + DLQ = handle delivery errors

Memory sentence: Scheduler = run by the hour, Event Bus = runs according to events.

AWS AppConfig

Purpose: Implement secure application configuration changes and feature flags with validation and automatic rollback without the need to build/deploy code again.

1. Protection layers during configuration rollout

ComponentProtection function
ValidatorsCheck syntax/semantics using JSON Schema or Lambda function before Enables configuration release
Deployment Strategy & Bake TimeRelease slowly at a rate (Linear, Canary) and then keep the follow-up period (Bake Time) after reaching 100%
CloudWatch Alarm + Auto RollbackAutomatically stops and returns to the previous stable configuration as soon as CloudWatch Alarm reports an increase in error rate/latency

2. Distinguish SSM Parameter Store vs AWS AppConfig

CriteriaSSM Parameter StoreAWS AppConfig
Main purposeStores configuration key-value pairs and secret strings (SecureString)Dynamic configuration distribution & feature flags Controlled operational risks
Validation, Gradual Rollout & Auto RollbackThere is no built-in canary/auto-rollback mechanism according to CloudWatch AlarmFull support schema/Lambda validation, canary/linear rollout, and automatic rollback
AWS Service Catalog, License Manager & AWS Managed Services (AMS)

Purpose: Standardize the self-provisioning of approved infrastructure, software licensing control, and outsourcing the operation of multi-account enterprise environments.

1. AWS Service Catalog & AWS License Manager

Administration serviceMechanism of action & Distinguishing point
AWS Service CatalogManage approved infrastructure portfolio (Product written in CloudFormation compiled in Portfolio attach IAM permissions and launch constraints) for groups to create themselves Provisioned Product compliant with security standards/tags without needing permission to create raw resources or write your own IaC
AWS License ManagerCentralized management of commercial licenses (Oracle, Windows/SQL Server, SAP...) on both AWS and On-premises via License Configuration count and block exceeding the limit vCPU, Cores, Sockets or Instances (integrates EC2 Launch, Dedicated Hosts, Organizations)
Platform Admin defines CloudFormation Template → puts it into Service Catalog Portfolio → Developer selects allowed Products → creates standard-compliant AWS Resources

2. AWS Managed Services (AMS)

AMS Model/ComponentOperating range
AMS AccelerateAdd 24/7 monitoring, incident detection, patching and backup on existing environments; The customer team still directly changes the infrastructure
AMS Advanced & MALZOperate strictly on Multi-Account Landing Zone (MALZ); Infrastructure changes go through the process RFC (Request for Change) or approved self-service
